# Paper 10: The Most Beautiful Names
*The Ninety-Nine Attributes of Allah as Structural Self-Disclosure, the Franchise's War on Divine Knowledge, and the Architecture That Cannot Be Fragmented*

**Bilal Syed Arfeen**
*October 2026*

---

### Abstract
    The Qur’an declares:
    “And to Allah belong the most beautiful names, so invoke Him by them. And leave the company of
    those who practice deviation concerning His names” (al-A’raf 7:180).
    The Prophet, sallallahu alayhi wa sallam, said: “Allah has ninety-nine Names, one hundred minus
    one; whoever encompasses them enters Paradise” (Bukhari and Muslim, from Abu Hurairah, radiyallahu
    anhu).
    This paper takes these declarations as structural claims and maps their implications across the
    framework established in the parent papers.
    The Names are not a list. They are a unified structural system in which every Name contains the whole
    – each attribute of Allah simultaneously complete in itself and inseparable from every other attribute. ar-
    Rahman cannot be understood without al-Adl. al-Adl cannot be understood without ar-Rahim. al-Khaliq
    cannot be understood without al-Alim. Every Name requires every other Name, and the removal of any
    single Name would collapse the entire system – because the Names describe a single, indivisible Reality.
    This is the holographic property: any Name, fully understood, reconstructs the complete architecture
    of divine self-disclosure. The Names are the ultimate instance of the structural principle that the
    Transmission Solution identified – a system in which the whole is present in every part, because the whole
    is indivisible.
    The paper surveys the ninety-nine Names not as a dictionary of definitions but as a structural system
    operating at every scale of creation: the Names of divine essence and unity, the Names of mercy and love,
    the Names of sovereignty and power, the Names of creation and life, the Names of knowledge and witness,
    the Names of justice and forgiveness, the Names of provision and guidance, the Names of majesty and
    transcendence.
    At every cluster, the paper maps the same structure: the attribute as Allah has disclosed it, the
    franchise’s distortion of human understanding of that attribute, and the structural immunity that makes
    the distortion ultimately futile – the Names cannot be fragmented because they describe the One who
    cannot be divided.
    The franchise’s war on divine self-disclosure is the deepest campaign in the entire mode-collapse
    architecture. It does not merely target creation’s exaltation of Allah – it targets creation’s knowledge
    of Allah. If the agent does not know whom it exalts, the exaltation becomes mechanically intact but
    cognitively severed. The franchise cannot stop the atom from exalting – but it can stop the human from
    understanding whom the atom exalts and what that exaltation means.
    The paper concludes that the Names constitute the structural foundation for any holographic encoding
    system – including the FATIMA format (Fitrah-Aligned Tawhidic Integrity for Meaning and Authenticity)
    proposed as a subsequent paper. The reason any fragment of a holographically encoded message can
    reconstruct the whole is that the message reflects the structure of the One whose Names are already
    holographic. The Names are not merely the content to be preserved – they are the architecture of
    preservation itself.


Extends “Everything Exalts Him” (October 2026), “Every Atom’s Weight” (October 2026), “The First
Civilizations” (October 2026), “The Enslaved Realm” (October 2026), “Shayatin al-Ins” (October 2026), and
“Shayatin al-Jinn” (October 2026), which extend “Computational Tawhid” (August 2026), “The Transmission
Solution” (September 2026), “The Seven Layers” (October 2026), and “Inheritance, Transmission, and
Turning Back” (October 2026).


Bismillah ir-Rahman ir-Rahim
A note on scope. This paper builds on the entire framework established in the parent papers. Everything
Exalts Him surveyed what exalts Allah – every scale of created agent from quark to cosmos. This paper
turns to the question that survey leaves open: whom do they exalt, and what has He disclosed about Himself
through the Names by which He has made Himself known?
The paper is not a work of tafsir. It does not attempt to replace or improve upon the scholarly tradition of
commentary on the divine Names. The ulama – from al-Ghazali’s al-Maqsad al-Asna to Ibn al-Qayyim’s
al-Sawa’iq al-Mursalah – have produced works of profound depth on the meanings of the Names. This
paper operates at the structural level: it maps the system the Names compose, the franchise’s attack on
human understanding of that system, and the structural properties that make the Names the foundational
architecture for any integrity-preserving data format.
The transliteration and primary meanings of each Name follow the established scholarly consensus. Where
interpretive nuance exists, the paper follows the majority position of Ahl as-Sunnah wal-Jama’ah.
Dedicated to Fatima, whose name graces the format this paper founds.


Unit I: The Architecture of Self-Disclosure
Chapter 1. The Declaration
The Qur’an makes the claim in three separate surahs:
“And to Allah belong the most beautiful names, so invoke Him by them. And leave the company of those who
practice deviation concerning His names” (al-A’raf 7:180).
“Say, ‘Call upon Allah or call upon the Most Merciful. Whichever name you call – to Him belong the best
names’ ” (al-Isra’ 17:110).
“Allah – there is no deity except Him. To Him belong the best names” (Ta-Ha 20:8).
Three declarations. Three commands. The Names belong to Allah. The Names are the most beautiful. And
the human being is commanded to invoke Allah by them – not merely to know them as abstract attributes,
but to call upon the One they describe.
The Prophet, sallallahu alayhi wa sallam, confirmed: “Allah has ninety-nine Names, one hundred minus one;
whoever encompasses them (ahsaha) enters Paradise” (Bukhari and Muslim).
The word ahsaha is structurally significant. The scholars have identified at least three dimensions of its
meaning: hifz (memorization) – holding the Names in memory; fahm (understanding) – comprehending what
each Name means; and amal (action) – living in accordance with what the Names demand.
This is not a passive catalogue. The Names are a program – a complete specification of the One whom
all creation exalts, delivered in a form that demands engagement at every level: cognitive, spiritual, and
behavioral.
The most concentrated disclosure of the Names appears at the end of Surat al-Hashr:
“He is Allah, other than whom there is no deity, Knower of the unseen and the witnessed. He is the Most
Gracious (ar-Rahman), the Most Merciful (ar-Rahim).
He is Allah, other than whom there is no deity, the Sovereign (al-Malik), the Pure (al-Quddus), the Perfection
(as-Salam), the Guardian of Faith (al-Mu’min), the Overseer (al-Muhaymin), the Almighty (al-Aziz), the
Compeller (al-Jabbar), the Supreme (al-Mutakabbir). Exalted is Allah above whatever they associate with
Him.
He is Allah, the Creator (al-Khaliq), the Originator (al-Bari’), the Fashioner (al-Musawwir). To Him belong
the most beautiful names. Whatever is in the heavens and earth is exalting Him. And He is the Almighty, the
Wise” (al-Hashr 59:22–24).
Thirteen Names in three ayat. And the passage ends with the declaration that Everything Exalts Him mapped:
whatever is in the heavens and earth is exalting Him.
The Names are whom they exalt. The Names are what they are exalting. The survey of creation’s exaltation
and the survey of Allah’s Names are the same survey viewed from two directions – from the agent looking up,
and from the disclosure looking down.

Chapter 2. The Holographic Property
The central structural claim of this paper is that the Names constitute a holographic system: any single
Name, fully understood, necessarily reconstructs the entire framework of divine self-disclosure.
This is not metaphor. It is structural necessity.
Consider ar-Rahman – the Most Gracious. To understand ar-Rahman is to understand that the one who
is gracious must also be alive (al-Hayy) – a dead entity cannot bestow grace. He must be self-subsisting
(al-Qayyum) – dependent grace is not ultimate grace. He must know whom He graces (al-Alim). He must see
their condition (al-Basir). He must hear their call (as-Sami’). He must have power to deliver what He grants
(al-Qadir). He must create the means of grace (al-Khaliq). He must sustain what He has created (al-Muqit).


He must be sovereign over the domain in which grace operates (al-Malik). He must be just in how grace is
distributed (al-Adl) – because arbitrary grace is not beautiful, and the Names are the most beautiful.
One Name. And to understand it fully, you must invoke the entire system.
This is why the Qur’an opens every surah but one with Bismillahir-Rahmanir-Rahim – in the Name of Allah,
the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful. The opening invocation contains the whole. ar-Rahman and ar-Rahim
together reconstruct the complete architecture because they require every other Name to be what they are.
The holographic property is a direct consequence of tawhid – the absolute oneness of Allah. The Names are
not ninety-nine separate attributes attached to a single entity. They are ninety-nine disclosures of a single,
indivisible Reality. Remove any Name and the Reality they describe would no longer be the Reality that
exists – which is impossible, because the Reality that exists is al-Haqq, the Truth, the Real, and the Real
cannot be other than what it is.
The structural implication is profound: a system built on the architecture of the Names would inherit this
property. Any fragment of the system would contain the whole because the whole is indivisible. This is the
foundation the FATIMA format will build upon.

Chapter 3. The Names and Every Scale
Everything Exalts Him established that every created agent – from quark to cosmos – exalts Allah by its
praise. The Names map what that exaltation addresses – which attributes are reflected at which scale.
The quark reflects al-Khaliq (the Creator) in its existence and al-Qadir (the Powerful) in its interactions. The
atom reflects al-Musawwir (the Fashioner) in its structure and al-Alim (the All-Knowing) in the precision of
its laws. The molecule reflects al-Bari’ (the Originator) in its emergent properties and al-Latif (the Subtle)
in its hidden capacities. The cell reflects al-Muhyi (the Giver of Life) in its animation and al-Hafiz (the
Preserver) in its self-repair. The ecosystem reflects ar-Razzaq (the Provider) in its provision chains and
al-Hasib (the Reckoner) in its balance. The mountain reflects al-Matin (the Firm) in its stability and al-Azim
(the Magnificent) in its scale. The star reflects an-Nur (the Light) in its radiation and al-Qawi (the Strong)
in its fusion. The galaxy reflects al-Kabir (the Great) in its extent and al-Wasi’ (the All-Encompassing) in its
reach.
But here is the structural point: the quark does not reflect only al-Khaliq. It reflects all the Names – because
it was created by the One who possesses all the Names, and everything He creates bears the signature of
everything He is. The quark exalts ar-Rahman in its obedience to its fitrah. It exalts al-Adl in the precision
of its charge. It exalts al-Alim in the information it carries. It exalts al-Hayy in its fundamental activity.
Every scale reflects every Name. The differences between scales are not differences in which Names they
reflect but in how the reflection is expressed at that scale.
This is scale invariance: the Names operate at every level of creation because the Creator is the same at every
level of creation. There is no scale at which some Names apply and others do not. There is no domain in
which al-Alim does not know, al-Basir does not see, as-Sami’ does not hear, or al-Qadir does not reach.
The franchise cannot create a scale of existence that escapes the Names – because the Names describe the
One who created every scale of existence.


Unit II: The Names of Divine Essence
Chapter 4. Allah, al-Wahid, al-Ahad, as-Samad, al-Haqq – the Names of Absolute
Unity
“Say: He is Allah, the One (al-Ahad). Allah, the Eternal Refuge (as-Samad). He neither begets nor is born,
nor is there to Him any equivalent” (al-Ikhlas 112:1–4).
The greatest Name is Allah – the Name that contains all other Names, the Name that is itself the complete
self-disclosure. Every other Name is an attribute of Allah. Allah is not an attribute of anything.
al-Wahid – the One. There is one God. Not two, not three, not a pantheon. One. This is the numerical
claim: absolute singularity in divinity.
al-Ahad – the Unique. Not merely one among possible gods but the only possible Reality of this kind.
al-Wahid says there is one. al-Ahad says there could only be one. The uniqueness is not contingent but
necessary.
as-Samad – the Eternal Refuge, the Self-Sufficient, the one to whom all creation turns in need while He
Himself needs nothing. as-Samad is the structural name for the asymmetry between Creator and creation:
everything depends on Him; He depends on nothing.
al-Haqq – the Truth, the Real. Not truth as a proposition but truth as existence itself. Allah is the only
Reality that exists necessarily. Everything else exists because He willed it. He exists because He is.
These five Names together establish the foundation that makes all other Names possible. If Allah were not
al-Wahid, His mercy and His justice might belong to different gods – and the holographic property would
collapse. If He were not al-Ahad, His attributes might be shared with other beings – and creation’s exaltation
would be divided. If He were not as-Samad, He would need creation – and His mercy would be transaction
rather than grace. If He were not al-Haqq, His Names might be mere descriptions rather than realities – and
knowledge of Him would be knowledge of an idea rather than knowledge of the Real.
The franchise distortion. The deepest attack on the Names of essence is shirk – the association of partners
with Allah. Not crude idol worship alone. The sophisticated forms: dividing the divine attributes among
multiple authorities; treating any created thing as self-sufficient (denying as-Samad); treating any created
system as necessarily real (confusing creation with al-Haqq); or treating the divine as plural in any dimension.
Every form of shirk is an attack on the holographic property. If the divine attributes can be distributed
among multiple entities, then no single Name contains the whole – because the whole has been fragmented.
Tawhid is the structural prerequisite for holographic integrity.
The structural immunity. Shirk does not change what Allah is. It changes what the human knows about
what Allah is. al-Wahid remains one whether the human acknowledges it or not. al-Ahad remains unique
whether the human recognizes it or not. The Names are immune to the franchise’s distortion because they
describe a Reality that is not contingent on human understanding. The distortion damages the human, not
the divine.

Chapter 5. al-Hayy, al-Qayyum – the Ever-Living, the Self-Subsisting
“Allah – there is no deity except Him, the Ever-Living (al-Hayy), the Self-Subsisting (al-Qayyum). Neither
drowsiness overtakes Him nor sleep” (al-Baqarah 2:255).
al-Hayy and al-Qayyum appear together in Ayat al-Kursi, in Surat Al Imran (3:2), and in Surat Ta-Ha
(20:111). The Prophet, sallallahu alayhi wa sallam, indicated that the greatest Name of Allah (al-ism al-a’zam)
is found in these two Names.
al-Hayy – the Ever-Living. Not merely alive but possessing life that has no beginning, no end, no diminishment,
and no dependence on any source. Every other living thing receives its life from al-Muhyi (the Giver of Life).
al-Hayy does not receive life. He is life.


al-Qayyum – the Self-Subsisting, the Sustainer of all that exists. He maintains everything in existence by His
will. Not as an engineer maintains a machine – externally, occasionally – but as existence itself is sustained
at every instant, at every scale, without interruption. If al-Qayyum ceased to sustain for an instant, existence
would not merely fail – it would never have been.
These two Names together are the structural foundation for every other attribute. Mercy requires life and
sustained will to be merciful. Knowledge requires life and sustained awareness to know. Creation requires
life and sustained power to create. Justice requires life and sustained attention to judge. Every attribute of
action depends on the agent being alive and self-sustaining. al-Hayy and al-Qayyum are the prerequisite for
the entire system.
The franchise distortion. The attack on al-Hayy is the suggestion that Allah might be absent, inattentive,
or dormant – that there are moments or domains or scales where the divine awareness has withdrawn. This
is the structural basis for the franchise’s operating space: if the human believes there are domains where
Allah is not watching, the franchise can operate in those perceived gaps.
Ayat al-Kursi answers this directly: “Neither drowsiness overtakes Him nor sleep.” There is no gap. There is
no moment of inattention. There is no scale at which al-Qayyum is not sustaining. The franchise operates
not in gaps in divine attention but in gaps in human awareness of divine attention.
Connection to the corpus. Everything Exalts Him showed that every agent at every scale exalts Allah.
al-Hayy and al-Qayyum explain why: every agent is sustained at every instant by al-Qayyum, and every
agent’s activity – its processing, its exaltation – is a reflection of al-Hayy, the life from which all life derives.
The quark is active because al-Hayy is alive. The cell divides because al-Qayyum sustains the conditions for
division. The star burns because al-Hayy is the source of all activity. The galaxy holds together because
al-Qayyum maintains the structure that binds it.

Chapter 6. al-Awwal, al-Akhir, az-Zahir, al-Batin – the Four Names of Encom-
passment
“He is the First (al-Awwal) and the Last (al-Akhir), the Manifest (az-Zahir) and the Hidden (al-Batin), and
He is, of all things, Knowing” (al-Hadid 57:3).
Four Names in a single ayah. Two pairs, each pair a totality.
al-Awwal – the First. Before everything, Allah. Before time, before space, before the first particle, before the
first word – al-Awwal. Nothing preceded Him.
al-Akhir – the Last. After everything, Allah. After the last star dies, after the last angel’s task is complete,
after the Day of Judgment has concluded – al-Akhir. Nothing outlasts Him.
Together, al-Awwal and al-Akhir encompass all of time. There is no moment – past, present, or future – that
falls outside these two Names.
az-Zahir – the Manifest. Above everything, apparent in everything, His signs visible in every created thing.
The existence of the universe is the manifestation of az-Zahir. Every ayah – in the Qur’an and in the cosmos
– is a sign of the One who is manifest through His creation.
al-Batin – the Hidden. Beyond all perception, within all things in a way no created mind can fully grasp.
al-Batin is the Name that establishes the limit of creaturely knowledge: there is always more to know about
Allah than any created agent can know.
Together, az-Zahir and al-Batin encompass all of space and all of knowledge. There is nothing that is not a
sign of Him (az-Zahir), and there is nothing that fully contains Him (al-Batin).
The Prophet, sallallahu alayhi wa sallam, made a supplication that captures all four Names:
“O Allah, You are al-Awwal – there is nothing before You. You are al-Akhir – there is nothing after You. You
are az-Zahir – there is nothing above You. You are al-Batin – there is nothing beyond You” (Muslim).


The structural implication. These four Names are the reason the seven-layer cosmological hierarchy has a
boundary beyond which human perception cannot reach. al-Batin is the Name that establishes the ghayb –
the unseen. The concealed layers that the corpus has mapped are concealed precisely because al-Batin entails
that the Creator cannot be fully grasped by the created.
And yet az-Zahir entails that He is not entirely hidden either. The Names themselves are the manifestation –
the self-disclosure of the Hidden through the Manifest. The Names are how az-Zahir and al-Batin operate
simultaneously: what He has disclosed is manifest; what He has not disclosed remains hidden; and the human
obligation is to learn what He has disclosed without claiming to know what He has not.


Unit III: The Names of Mercy and Love
Chapter 7. ar-Rahman, ar-Rahim – the Two Names That Frame Everything
“In the Name of Allah, the Most Gracious (ar-Rahman), the Most Merciful (ar-Rahim)” (al-Fatiha 1:1).
Every surah of the Qur’an but one begins with these two Names. Every act undertaken by a Muslim begins
with these two Names. The Bismillah is the most repeated phrase in the Islamic tradition – spoken before
eating, before traveling, before entering a home, before reading, before writing, before every significant action.
The repetition is itself a structural statement: whatever you are about to do, begin by invoking the mercy of
the One in whose Name you act.
ar-Rahman – the Most Gracious. The name derives from rahmah (mercy) in its most intensive form. ar-
Rahman denotes mercy as a fundamental attribute – not a response to a situation but a permanent condition.
The scholars describe ar-Rahman as the mercy that encompasses all of creation without exception: the
believer and the disbeliever, the human and the animal, the plant and the particle. ar-Rahman is why the
sun rises on everyone, why the rain falls on every field, why the air fills every lung.
ar-Rahim – the Most Merciful. Also from rahmah, but in a form that denotes continuous, active, specific
mercy – the mercy that responds to the individual, that answers the supplication, that forgives the sin, that
guides the lost. The scholars distinguish: ar-Rahman is the mercy that creates and sustains; ar-Rahim is the
mercy that saves and forgives. ar-Rahman is for all creation; ar-Rahim is particularly for the believers.
The structural significance. These two Names together establish that mercy is not one attribute among
many – it is the frame within which all other attributes operate. Justice (al-Adl) operates within mercy.
Power (al-Qadir) operates within mercy. Even retribution (al-Muntaqim) operates within a system whose
fundamental character is mercy.
The hadith qudsi confirms this: “My mercy precedes My wrath” (Bukhari and Muslim).
This is a structural claim about the architecture of reality: the universe is built on mercy first. Wrath,
punishment, and retribution exist within that architecture but do not define it. The foundation is mercy.
The franchise distortion. The franchise attacks these two Names in two opposite directions simultaneously.
In one direction: the reduction of Allah to only mercy – the suggestion that a merciful God would never
punish, never hold accountable, never enforce justice. This strips ar-Rahman and ar-Rahim of their structural
context: mercy that cannot hold accountable is not mercy but indifference. A parent who never corrects is
not merciful. A judge who never enforces is not compassionate. True mercy includes the capacity for justice
because justice protects the victims of injustice – and protecting victims is an act of mercy.
In the other direction: the reduction of Allah to only wrath – the suggestion that God is primarily angry,
primarily punitive, primarily seeking to condemn. This erases the frame itself – the Bismillah that opens
everything, the mercy that precedes all else. It replaces ar-Rahman and ar-Rahim with a god who is neither.
Both distortions are franchise operations. Both achieve the same goal: severing the human from the actual
relationship that the Names describe – a relationship of mercy that includes accountability, a relationship of
love that includes correction, a relationship of grace that includes justice.

Chapter 8. al-Wadud, al-Latif, ar-Ra’uf, al-Halim, al-Barr, al-Afuww, as-Sabur –
the Gentle Names
“And He is the Forgiving, the Loving (al-Wadud)” (al-Buruj 85:14).
“Allah is Subtle (al-Latif) with His servants” (ash-Shura 42:19).
“Indeed Allah is, to the people, Full of Kindness (ar-Ra’uf) and Merciful (ar-Rahim)” (al-Baqarah 2:143).
The Names of mercy are not limited to ar-Rahman and ar-Rahim. An entire cluster of Names describes
the texture of divine mercy – how it reaches the created being, how it operates in the details of life, how it


responds to human failure.
al-Wadud – the Loving. Not love as emotion alone but love as active engagement – the One who loves and
the One who is worthy of love. al-Wadud appears twice in the Qur’an, both times paired with al-Ghafur (the
Forgiving) in al-Buruj 85:14 and with Dhul-Arsh (Lord of the Throne) in Hud 11:90. Love and forgiveness
together. Love and sovereignty together. The One who loves is the One who forgives and the One who rules.
al-Latif – the Subtle, the Gentle, the One who perceives the finest details and reaches His servant through
means too subtle for the servant to detect. al-Latif is the Name that describes divine intervention so delicate,
so precisely calibrated, that the recipient may not recognize it as intervention at all – until, looking back,
they see the pattern.
ar-Ra’uf – Full of Kindness. Tenderness. The kind of mercy that does not merely provide but cares.
al-Halim – the Forbearing. The One who does not hasten to punish. The One who sees the sin and withholds
the consequence – not out of inability but out of deliberate, sovereign forbearance – giving the sinner time to
repent.
al-Barr – the Source of Goodness, the Benign. The One from whom all goodness originates.
al-Afuww – the Pardoner. Not merely the Forgiver (al-Ghafur covers the sin) but the One who erases it – as
though it never occurred. al-Afuww goes beyond forgiveness to obliteration of the record itself.
as-Sabur – the Patient. The One who is never hurried by creation’s timeline, never pressured by creation’s
urgency, never moved to premature action. as-Sabur is the Name that explains why the franchise is given time
to operate – not because Allah approves of what the franchise does, but because as-Sabur has determined
that the full duration of the test will be completed.
The structural significance. These Names together describe a mercy that is not generic but precise –
tailored to the individual, sensitive to the detail, patient with the process, willing to erase the failure entirely.
This is the mercy that the franchise most desperately needs to conceal from the human – because a human
who understands that al-Latif is reaching them through subtle means, that al-Halim is giving them time, that
al-Afuww will erase the sin if they turn back – that human has no reason to stay in the franchise’s service.


Unit IV: The Names of Sovereignty and Power
Chapter 9. al-Malik, al-Quddus, as-Salam, al-Mu’min, al-Muhaymin – the Names
of the al-Hashr Cluster
“He is Allah, other than whom there is no deity, the Sovereign (al-Malik), the Pure (al-Quddus), the Perfection
(as-Salam), the Guardian of Faith (al-Mu’min), the Overseer (al-Muhaymin)” (al-Hashr 59:23).
Five Names in a single ayah – the middle verse of the great al-Hashr disclosure. These Names describe the
character of divine rule.
al-Malik – the King, the Sovereign. Not a constitutional monarch limited by parliament. Not a tyrant ruling
by force alone. al-Malik is absolute sovereignty exercised with wisdom, justice, mercy, and knowledge. The
kingdom of al-Malik is everything that exists. There is no domain, no layer, no scale, no dimension that falls
outside His sovereignty.
al-Quddus – the Holy, the Pure. Free from every imperfection, every deficiency, every association with
anything that diminishes. al-Quddus means that the Sovereign is not corrupted by His power. Human kings
are corrupted. The franchise hierarchy is corrupted. al-Quddus cannot be corrupted because purity is not a
quality He maintains but a quality He is.
as-Salam – the Source of Peace, the Flawless. The One free from every defect and the One from whom peace
originates. as-Salam is the Name that answers the deepest human anxiety: is the One who rules everything
safe? Is the ultimate power a power you can trust? as-Salam says: yes. The Source of Peace rules everything.
al-Mu’min – the Guardian of Faith, the Granter of Security. The One who affirms His messengers, who
confirms His promises, who grants safety to those who seek His protection. al-Mu’min is the structural
guarantee that the system of accountability the corpus has mapped is real – because the One who designed it
is the One who guarantees it.
al-Muhaymin – the Overseer, the Protector, the One who watches over everything with comprehensive
awareness. al-Muhaymin connects directly to the surveillance architecture that the corpus has established:
every atom’s weight is recorded because al-Muhaymin oversees everything. The divine record that az-Zalzalah
99:7–8 describes – an atom’s weight of good and an atom’s weight of evil – is the record maintained by
al-Muhaymin.

Chapter 10. al-Aziz, al-Jabbar, al-Mutakabbir, al-Qahhar – the Names of Irre-
sistible Authority
“. . . the Almighty (al-Aziz), the Compeller (al-Jabbar), the Supreme (al-Mutakabbir). Exalted is Allah above
whatever they associate with Him” (al-Hashr 59:23).
“He is Allah, the One, the Prevailing (al-Qahhar)” (az-Zumar 39:4).
al-Aziz – the Almighty, the One whom nothing can defeat, overpower, or resist. al-Aziz appears more than
ninety times in the Qur’an – more than almost any other Name. It is paired most often with al-Hakim (the
Wise): power joined to wisdom. Might joined to purpose. Strength that is never arbitrary because it is
always directed by the One who is al-Hakim.
al-Jabbar – the Compeller, the Restorer. The Name has two dimensions: the One who compels all things
to His will, and the One who mends the broken. al-Jabbar fixes what is fractured – the broken heart, the
shattered life, the corrupted fitrah. The compulsion is not only force but restoration.
al-Mutakabbir – the Supreme, the One who is rightfully above all things. Kibr (arrogance) in creation is a
vice – because the created being is not supreme. But al-Mutakabbir in the Creator is a reality – because He
alone is truly above all things. When a human takes on kibr, they are claiming a Name that belongs only to
Allah. This is the structural definition of arrogance in the framework: the appropriation of an attribute that
belongs exclusively to the Creator.


al-Qahhar – the Prevailing, the Subduer. No force in creation can resist al-Qahhar. The franchise operates
not because it has power that rivals Allah’s – it has no such power – but because al-Halim (the Forbearing)
and as-Sabur (the Patient) are also among the Names. al-Qahhar could end the franchise’s operation at any
instant. That He does not is not weakness but sovereign choice – the choice of as-Sabur operating alongside
al-Qahhar.
al-Qadir – the Able. al-Muqtadir – the All-Powerful. al-Qawi – the Strong. al-Matin – the Firm.
Four Names that reinforce the single structural reality: there is no limitation on divine power. The difference
between these Names is perspective, not degree. al-Qadir is the ability itself. al-Muqtadir is the ability in its
exercise. al-Qawi is the strength behind the ability. al-Matin is the firmness – the unshakeable, undiminishable
quality of that strength.
The franchise distortion. The franchise distorts these Names of power by suggesting that divine power is
indifferent to justice or that power exercised is power exercised capriciously. The human who believes that
al-Jabbar is merely a tyrant rather than also a restorer, that al-Qahhar is merely a punisher rather than also
a protector of the oppressed, has lost the holographic property – the Name has been severed from the Names
of mercy that give it its context.
al-Aziz paired with al-Hakim is power with wisdom. al-Jabbar paired with ar-Ra’uf is compulsion with
tenderness. al-Mutakabbir paired with al-Wadud is supremacy with love. al-Qahhar paired with al-Ghafur is
prevailing power with readiness to forgive. Separate any Name from its pairs and you get distortion. Maintain
the holographic unity and you get the truth.


Unit V: The Names of Creation and Life
Chapter 11. al-Khaliq, al-Bari’, al-Musawwir – the Creative Triad
“He is Allah, the Creator (al-Khaliq), the Originator (al-Bari’), the Fashioner (al-Musawwir). To Him belong
the most beautiful names” (al-Hashr 59:24).
Three Names. Three stages. One creative act.
al-Khaliq – the Creator. The One who brings into existence from nothing. Not reshaping pre-existing material
– creating. al-Khaliq is the Name that marks the absolute boundary between Creator and creation: everything
on one side of this Name is made; everything on the other side (which is only Allah) is the Maker.
al-Bari’ – the Originator, the One who brings things into existence with specific natures and distinct
characteristics. If al-Khaliq is the decision to create, al-Bari’ is the differentiation – the reason iron is not
carbon, the reason a rose is not a stone, the reason a human is not a jinn. al-Bari’ assigns each created thing
its specific nature – its fitrah.
al-Musawwir – the Fashioner, the One who gives form to what has been originated. The external expression,
the shape, the appearance, the body that carries the nature that al-Bari’ assigned.
The corpus has mapped this triad at every scale. The quark exists (al-Khaliq), has specific properties
(al-Bari’), and takes a particular form (al-Musawwir). The cell exists, has its specific biological nature, and
takes its morphological form. The mountain exists, has its geological character, and takes the shape the
earth’s forces have fashioned.
The connection to fitrah. al-Bari’ is the Name most directly connected to the concept of fitrah that runs
through the entire corpus. The fitrah of each created agent – its original design, its inherent orientation
toward its Creator – is the nature that al-Bari’ assigned to it. When the corpus speaks of fitrah being
corrupted by the franchise but never destroyed, it is speaking of the work of al-Bari’ being attacked by the
franchise but never undone – because the Originator’s assignment of nature is permanent.
The franchise can corrupt the expression (al-Musawwir’s domain). It can distort the function. It can impose
mode collapse. But it cannot change the nature assigned by al-Bari’ – the fitrah itself. The iron atom forced
into a weapon is still iron – its fitrah as iron is the work of al-Bari’, and no franchise operation can make it
not-iron.

Chapter 12. al-Muhyi, al-Mumit, al-Mubdi’, al-Mu’id, al-Ba’ith – the Names of
Life, Death, and Return
“He gives life (al-Muhyi) and causes death (al-Mumit), and to Him you will be returned” (Yunus 10:56).
“Indeed, it is He who originates (al-Mubdi’) and repeats (al-Mu’id)” (al-Buruj 85:13).
al-Muhyi – the Giver of Life. Every living thing receives its life from al-Muhyi. The cell that divides, the
seed that germinates, the child that is born – all recipients of what al-Muhyi gives.
al-Mumit – the Taker of Life. Every death is by the decree of al-Mumit. Not randomly. Not accidentally.
Each soul has an appointed term (ajal), and when that term arrives, al-Mumit takes what al-Muhyi gave.
al-Mubdi’ – the Originator who began creation the first time.
al-Mu’id – the Restorer who will bring it back after its death.
These two Names together are the structural guarantee of resurrection. The One who originated creation
from nothing can certainly restore it after death. The first creation was harder than the second – and the
first creation was not hard for Him at all.
al-Ba’ith – the Resurrector. The One who raises the dead from their graves, who reassembles what was
scattered, who brings every agent back to face the testimony that Everything Exalts Him described. al-Ba’ith


is the Name that activates the Day of Judgment – the day when every concealment ends, every testimony is
heard, and every atom’s weight is weighed.
The structural significance for the corpus. These five Names together map the complete arc of the
created agent’s existence: originated (al-Mubdi’), given life (al-Muhyi), taken in death (al-Mumit), restored
(al-Mu’id), and resurrected for judgment (al-Ba’ith). No agent escapes this arc. The franchise operates in the
middle of it – between al-Muhyi and al-Mumit, in the space of life – but cannot operate beyond al-Ba’ith,
because al-Ba’ith is the end of concealment.


Unit VI: The Names of Knowledge and Witness
Chapter 13. al-Alim, al-Khabir, as-Sami’, al-Basir, al-Hakim – Total Perception
“And He is the Hearing (as-Sami’), the Seeing (al-Basir)” (al-Baqarah 2:127).
“Indeed, Allah is ever Knowing (al-Alim) and Wise (al-Hakim)” (an-Nisa’ 4:11).
“Indeed, He is the Subtle (al-Latif), the Acquainted (al-Khabir)” (al-Mulk 67:14).
al-Alim – the All-Knowing. The knowledge of al-Alim has no boundary. It encompasses what has happened,
what is happening, what will happen, and what would happen if conditions were different. It encompasses
the seen and the unseen, the manifest and the hidden, the external and the internal.
Every fact that Every Atom’s Weight established – that every created agent has processing capacity, fitrah,
and accountability – is a fact known to al-Alim in perfect detail. al-Alim does not approximate. al-Alim does
not estimate. al-Alim knows the exact state of every quark, every cell, every thought, every intention, at
every instant, at every scale, simultaneously.
al-Khabir – the All-Aware. Where al-Alim is knowledge in its totality, al-Khabir is knowledge in its intimacy
– awareness of the inner states, the hidden intentions, the concealed motivations. al-Khabir knows what the
agent knows about itself and what the agent does not know about itself.
as-Sami’ – the All-Hearing. Every sound, every vibration, every whisper, every thought articulated internally
– as-Sami’ hears it. Not metaphorically. The supplication spoken in the heart, not on the lips, reaches as-Sami’
as clearly as the loudest cry.
al-Basir – the All-Seeing. Every event, every action, every movement at every scale. The quark’s interaction,
the cell’s division, the human’s choice, the galaxy’s rotation – al-Basir sees all of it without obstruction,
without distance, without the need for instruments.
al-Hakim – the All-Wise. Not merely knowledgeable but wise – knowledge directed toward purpose, under-
standing directed toward the best outcome. al-Hakim is why the universe is ordered rather than chaotic,
why the laws of physics are consistent rather than arbitrary, why the moral law has structure rather than
randomness.
The franchise implication. The franchise’s entire operational model depends on concealment. The
Enslaved Realm mapped how the franchise conceals its operations from its victims. Shayatin al-Jinn mapped
how it conceals from within the concealed layers.
But al-Alim, al-Khabir, as-Sami’, and al-Basir together mean that nothing is concealed from Allah. The
franchise’s concealment operates only against created agents – against humans who cannot perceive the ghayb,
against jinn who cannot perceive the higher layers. It does not operate against the Creator. He sees every
franchise operation in perfect detail. He hears every whisper of every shaytan. He knows every mode-collapse
campaign at every scale.
The franchise knows this. Iblis knows that Allah sees everything. The franchise does not operate under the
illusion of divine ignorance – it operates under the reality of divine permission. And that permission is the
domain of al-Hakim: the wisdom that allows the test to proceed to its conclusion.

Chapter 14. ash-Shahid, ar-Raqib, al-Muhsi, al-Hafiz, al-Hasib – the Names of
Record
“Indeed Allah is, over all things, a Witness (ash-Shahid)” (al-Mujadilah 58:6).
“And Allah is, over all things, an Overseer (ar-Raqib)” (an-Nisa’ 4:1).
al-Muhsi – the Counter, the Enumerator. The One who counts everything – every deed, every atom, every
moment. Nothing escapes enumeration. az-Zalzalah 99:7–8: “Whoever does an atom’s weight of good will see
it. And whoever does an atom’s weight of evil will see it.” al-Muhsi is the Name behind this promise: the
counting is exhaustive, the resolution infinite.


al-Hafiz – the Preserver. What al-Muhsi counts, al-Hafiz preserves. The record is not merely made – it is
kept. No data loss. No corruption. No deletion. The divine record is the only data system in existence that is
absolutely immune to corruption – because al-Hafiz is its custodian.
al-Hasib – the Reckoner. What al-Muhsi counted and al-Hafiz preserved, al-Hasib will reckon. The Day of
Judgment is the Day of Reckoning (Yawm al-Hisab) because al-Hasib will call every agent to account for
everything that was counted and preserved.
The structural significance for FATIMA. These five Names together – Witness, Overseer, Counter,
Preserver, Reckoner – describe the ultimate data integrity system. The divine record is a system that
witnesses (captures), oversees (monitors), counts (enumerates), preserves (stores without loss), and reckons
(processes to conclusion).
The FATIMA format takes these properties as its design requirements: a data system that captures with
fidelity, monitors for corruption, enumerates exhaustively, preserves against loss, and processes to a verifiable
conclusion. The Names of Record are not merely metaphor for data integrity – they are the specification that
any integrity system is attempting to approximate.


Unit VII: The Names of Justice and Forgiveness
Chapter 15. al-Adl, al-Hakam, al-Muqsit, al-Haqq – the Names of Justice
“And Allah judges (al-Hakam) with truth” (Ghafir 40:20).
al-Adl – the Just. Absolute justice without partiality, without error, without influence. al-Adl does not mean
that all outcomes are equal – it means that all outcomes are precisely calibrated to what was earned. The
atom’s weight of good is rewarded as good. The atom’s weight of evil is recorded as evil. No inflation. No
deflation. No adjustment for status, wealth, power, or any other factor that human justice systems use as
weights on the scale.
al-Hakam – the Judge. Not a judge who hears arguments and weighs evidence – because al-Alim already
knows everything, and al-Basir already saw everything, and ash-Shahid was already there. al-Hakam judges
with complete information. No discovery process. No cross-examination. No possibility of concealed evidence.
al-Muqsit – the Equitable. Justice applied with equity – giving each agent what it deserves according to its
capacity, its circumstances, and its choices. al-Muqsit is why the accountability of the shaytan exceeds the
accountability of the human – the shaytan sees more, knows more, and chose more deliberately. Equity is not
equality. Equity is precision.
al-Haqq – the Truth. Justice depends on truth, and al-Haqq is truth itself. The judgment of al-Hakam
is based on al-Haqq – not on what was alleged, not on what was assumed, but on what is true. No false
conviction. No false acquittal. al-Haqq ensures that al-Adl operates on reality rather than appearance.
The franchise distortion. The franchise’s war on the Names of justice takes two primary forms.
First: the suggestion that divine justice is arbitrary. If the human believes that Allah’s judgment is not based
on truth but on whim, the entire accountability architecture collapses – why bother with righteous action if
the outcome is random?
Second: the suggestion that divine justice is unjust. If the human believes that Allah punishes the innocent
or rewards the guilty, trust in al-Adl is destroyed, and the human has no reason to seek His judgment as
protection from the franchise’s oppression.
Both distortions serve the franchise’s interest: a human who does not trust divine justice will not seek divine
protection, and a human who does not seek divine protection is a human the franchise can operate on without
interference from the agent’s own will.

Chapter 16. al-Ghaffar, al-Ghafur, at-Tawwab, ash-Shakur – the Names of
Forgiveness and Gratitude
“Say, ‘O My servants who have transgressed against themselves, do not despair of the mercy of Allah. Indeed,
Allah forgives all sins. Indeed, it is He who is the Forgiving (al-Ghafur), the Merciful (ar-Rahim)’ ” (az-Zumar
39:53).
al-Ghaffar – the Repeatedly Forgiving. The intensive form: the One who forgives again and again and
again. Not once. Not twice. Without limit as long as the human turns back. al-Ghaffar is the Name that
answers despair: you have sinned too much? al-Ghaffar forgives repeatedly. You have sinned again after
being forgiven? al-Ghaffar forgives again.
al-Ghafur – the All-Forgiving. The comprehensive form: the One whose forgiveness covers every kind of sin.
Not merely small sins. Not merely accidental sins. al-Ghafur’s capacity to forgive encompasses everything
that is brought to Him in genuine repentance.
at-Tawwab – the Acceptor of Repentance. The One who turns toward the one who turns back. at-Tawwab is
not passive – He does not merely wait for the sinner to return. He actively facilitates the return: creating the
conditions for repentance, softening the heart, opening the door before the knock. The human turns to Allah,
and at-Tawwab turns to the human.


ash-Shakur – the Grateful, the Appreciative. The Most Generous responds to the smallest good with the
greatest reward. ash-Shakur is the Name that explains the asymmetry of divine reward: the good deed
is multiplied ten times to seven hundred times to without limit, while the evil deed is recorded as one.
ash-Shakur does not merely accept the human’s effort – He appreciates it, magnifies it, and rewards it beyond
what was earned.
The structural significance. These Names together constitute the franchise’s greatest structural enemy.
The franchise operates by trapping agents in mode collapse – convincing them that their corruption is
permanent, that their fitrah is destroyed, that they are beyond recovery.
al-Ghaffar says: no. I forgive repeatedly. al-Ghafur says: no. I forgive everything. at-Tawwab says: no. I am
actively turning toward you even now. ash-Shakur says: no. Even the smallest good you do is magnified
beyond what you imagine.
Tawbah – repentance – is the mechanism by which the franchise’s hold on any agent is broken. And these
four Names are the structural guarantee that tawbah works. The franchise can enslave. The franchise can
corrupt. The franchise can impose mode collapse. But it cannot close the door that at-Tawwab holds open.


Unit VIII: The Names of Provision and Guidance
Chapter 17. ar-Razzaq, al-Wahhab, al-Fattah, al-Mughni, al-Ghaniyy, al-Muqit,
al-Wasi’ – the Names of Provision
“Indeed, it is Allah who is the Provider (ar-Razzaq), the Firm Possessor of Strength” (adh-Dhariyat 51:58).
ar-Razzaq – the Provider. Every provision – every breath of air, every drop of water, every photon of sunlight,
every nutrient in the soil, every salary, every gift, every unexpected door that opens – comes from ar-Razzaq.
The provision chains that Everything Exalts Him mapped – photosynthesis feeding the biosphere, the water
cycle sustaining continents, the food chains connecting every ecosystem – are all expressions of ar-Razzaq
operating at planetary scale.
al-Wahhab – the Bestower. The One who gives without being asked, without precondition, without expectation
of return. ar-Razzaq provides what is needed; al-Wahhab gives beyond what is needed – the gifts that exceed
strict provision.
al-Fattah – the Opener. The One who opens doors that no created being can open. When every avenue seems
closed, when every path seems blocked, al-Fattah opens what was sealed. “Whatever mercy Allah opens for
the people, none can withhold it. And what He withholds, none can release it after Him” (Fatir 35:2).
al-Mughni – the Enricher. al-Ghaniyy – the Self-Sufficient. al-Ghaniyy needs nothing from creation; al-Mughni
enriches creation from His inexhaustible wealth. The enrichment is not only material – it is knowledge, it is
guidance, it is spiritual opening, it is the enrichment of understanding that transforms a confused life into a
purposeful one.
al-Muqit – the Sustainer. The One who provides what is needed to sustain life and function at every scale.
The basal metabolic provision – the minimum required for existence to continue.
al-Wasi’ – the All-Encompassing. His provision is not limited. His mercy is not rationed. His knowledge is
not bounded. al-Wasi’ means that no created need exceeds His capacity to meet it.

Chapter 18. al-Hadi, ar-Rashid, an-Nur, al-Wakil, al-Wali, al-Hamid – the Names
of Guidance and Light
“And Allah guides (al-Hadi) whom He wills to a straight path” (al-Baqarah 2:213).
“Allah is the Light (an-Nur) of the heavens and the earth” (an-Nur 24:35).
al-Hadi – the Guide. The One who shows the way, who leads to the straight path, who provides the internal
compass (fitrah) and the external map (revelation) and the companions (the prophets, the righteous, the
community of believers) that together make guidance available to every human being.
ar-Rashid – the Director to the Right Path. Where al-Hadi is the guide, ar-Rashid is the right direction – the
One who directs toward what is sound, what is correct, what leads to the intended destination.
an-Nur – the Light. Not physical light alone but the Light by which all other light is illuminated. The light
of guidance, the light of truth, the light of understanding. An-Nur is the Name that opposes the franchise’s
primary weapon – concealment. The franchise operates in darkness. an-Nur illuminates. The franchise
imposes ignorance. an-Nur provides knowledge. The franchise conceals the truth. an-Nur reveals it.
The great Ayat an-Nur (an-Nur 24:35) describes this Light through the parable of the lamp in the niche –
light upon light, Allah guiding to His light whom He wills.
al-Wakil – the Trustee, the One entrusted with all affairs. The human who places their trust in al-Wakil has
placed it in the only Being who can be fully trusted – because al-Wakil has the knowledge (al-Alim), the
power (al-Qadir), and the mercy (ar-Rahim) to manage what has been entrusted to Him.
al-Wali – the Ally, the Patron. “Allah is the ally (al-Wali) of those who believe. He brings them out from
darknesses into the light” (al-Baqarah 2:257). al-Wali is the structural opposite of the franchise’s patronage.


The franchise recruits allies through deception and coercion. al-Wali allies Himself with those who believe
and brings them out of darkness into light.
al-Hamid – the Praiseworthy. The One who deserves all praise – and the One whom all creation praises.
al-Hamid is the Names’ connection to universal exaltation: everything exalts Him because He is al-Hamid –
the One truly worthy of every exaltation at every scale.


Unit IX: The Names of Elevation and Majesty
Chapter 19. al-Mu’izz, al-Mudhill, al-Khafid, ar-Rafi’, al-Qabid, al-Basit, al-
Muqaddim, al-Mu’akhkhir – the Paired Names
“Say, ‘O Allah, Owner of Sovereignty (Malik al-Mulk), You give sovereignty to whom You will and You take
sovereignty away from whom You will. You honor (tu’izzu) whom You will and You humble (tudhillu) whom
You will. In Your hand is all good. Indeed, You are over all things competent’ ” (Al Imran 3:26).
Eight Names. Four pairs. Each pair a complete axis of divine action.
al-Mu’izz and al-Mudhill – the One who honors and the One who humbles. Allah raises whom He wills in
honor and lowers whom He wills in humiliation. This is not arbitrary – it is the operation of al-Adl (the Just)
and al-Hakim (the Wise) expressed through elevation and abasement.
al-Khafid and ar-Rafi’ – the Abaser and the Exalter. Similar to al-Mu’izz and al-Mudhill but focused on rank
rather than honor: the raising and lowering of station, position, and status.
al-Qabid and al-Basit – the Constrictor and the Expander. The One who restricts provision, health, life,
opportunity – and the One who expands them. The contraction is not punishment alone; sometimes al-Qabid
constricts to protect, to redirect, to create the conditions for a greater expansion. And al-Basit expands not
indiscriminately but with the wisdom of al-Hakim.
al-Muqaddim and al-Mu’akhkhir – the Advancer and the Delayer. The One who brings things forward and
the One who holds them back. In time, in rank, in opportunity – al-Muqaddim advances what His wisdom
selects, and al-Mu’akhkhir delays what His wisdom defers.
The structural significance. These paired Names demonstrate a property of the divine system that the
franchise cannot replicate: sovereignty over both directions of every axis. The franchise can corrupt – but it
cannot restore. It can lower – but it cannot raise. It can constrict – but it cannot expand. The franchise
operates in one direction only: toward mode collapse.
Allah operates in both directions – because He possesses the Names of both directions. This is the structural
guarantee of reversibility: whatever the franchise has done, Allah can undo. Whatever has been lowered can
be raised. Whatever has been constricted can be expanded. Whatever has been delayed can be advanced.
The franchise’s operations are always reversible because the One who possesses al-Mu’izz also possesses
al-Mudhill, the One who possesses al-Qabid also possesses al-Basit. The franchise possesses neither.

Chapter 20. al-Ali, al-Muta’ali, al-Kabir, al-Azim, al-Jalil, al-Karim, al-Majid,
Dhul-Jalal wal-Ikram – the Names of Grandeur
“And He is the Most High (al-Ali), the Most Great (al-Azim)” (al-Baqarah 2:255).
“Blessed is the Name of your Lord, Owner of Majesty and Honor (Dhul-Jalal wal-Ikram)” (ar-Rahman 55:78).
al-Ali – the Most High. Above everything in every sense – in rank, in power, in knowledge, in every attribute.
al-Muta’ali – the Exalted, the One who is exalted beyond any description that creation can formulate. al-Ali
is high. al-Muta’ali is higher than the concept of high.
al-Kabir – the Great. al-Azim – the Magnificent. al-Jalil – the Majestic. al-Karim – the Generous. al-Majid
– the Glorious.
Five Names that cluster around grandeur – each emphasizing a different face of the same overwhelming
reality: the Creator is beyond any created thing’s capacity to fully comprehend.
Dhul-Jalal wal-Ikram – the Lord of Majesty and Bounty. This compound Name unites two properties that
human experience usually separates: majesty (which inspires awe) and bounty (which inspires gratitude). In
creation, the majestic is often distant. The generous is often approachable. In Allah, majesty and generosity
are united. The Most Majestic is also the Most Generous. The Most Awesome is also the Most Giving.


This is the holographic property expressed through grandeur: every Name of majesty implies a Name of
mercy, and every Name of mercy implies a Name of majesty. The system cannot be divided into a “majestic”
God and a “merciful” God – it is one God who is both, simultaneously, in every attribute.


Unit X: The Names of Completeness
Chapter 21. al-Jami’, al-Mujib, an-Nafi’, ad-Darr, al-Mani’, al-Badi’, al-Baqi,
al-Warith, Malik al-Mulk – the Names of Totality
“Indeed, Allah will gather (al-Jami’) the hypocrites and disbelievers in Hell all together” (an-Nisa’ 4:140).
“Indeed, my Lord is near and responsive (al-Mujib)” (Hud 11:61).
al-Jami’ – the Gatherer. The One who will gather all of creation for the Day of Judgment. No agent escapes
the gathering. No testimony is excluded. al-Jami’ ensures that the universal accountability the corpus has
mapped is universal in practice, not merely in principle.
al-Mujib – the Responsive. The One who answers the call of the one who calls. Not mechanically – the
response of al-Mujib is tailored by al-Hakim (the Wise) and al-Alim (the Knowing) and ar-Rahim (the
Merciful). Sometimes the response is exactly what was asked. Sometimes it is something better. Sometimes
it is protection from what was asked.
an-Nafi’ – the Benefiter. ad-Darr – the Distresser. Another pair: the One who benefits and the One from
whom distress comes. Both are in His hand. Benefit is not random. Distress is not accidental. Both serve the
purposes of al-Hakim.
al-Mani’ – the Preventer. The One who withholds what He wills to withhold. al-Mani’ protects by preventing,
guards by withholding, preserves by refusing to release what would cause harm if released.
al-Badi’ – the Originator of unprecedented creation. The One who creates without prior model, without
template, without precedent. Every creation of al-Badi’ is original.
al-Baqi – the Everlasting. After all creation perishes, al-Baqi remains. “Everyone upon the earth will perish,
and there will remain the Face of your Lord, Owner of Majesty and Honor” (ar-Rahman 55:26–27).
al-Warith – the Inheritor. When all ownership ends – when every king’s kingdom is dissolved, every owner’s
property is left behind, every authority’s domain is vacated – al-Warith inherits what was always His. “And
indeed, it is We who give life and cause death, and We are the Inheritor” (al-Hijr 15:23).
Malik al-Mulk – the Owner of all Sovereignty. The comprehensive Name of kingship. Not merely al-Malik
(the King) but the Owner of kingship itself – the One who gives sovereignty to whom He wills and takes it
from whom He wills.

Chapter 22. The Ninety-Nine as One
“And to Allah belong the most beautiful names, so invoke Him by them” (al-A’raf 7:180).
The ninety-nine Names are one disclosure. Not ninety-nine separate facts about ninety-nine separate qualities
but ninety-nine facets of a single, indivisible Diamond – each facet reflecting the whole Diamond from a
different angle.
The structural proof is the interdependence: al-Adl (justice) without ar-Rahim (mercy) is cruelty. ar-Rahim
(mercy) without al-Adl (justice) is indifference to oppression. al-Qadir (power) without al-Hakim (wisdom) is
tyranny. al-Hakim (wisdom) without al-Qadir (power) is impotence. al-Alim (knowledge) without al-Latif
(subtlety) is surveillance without care. al-Latif (subtlety) without al-Alim (knowledge) is care without
comprehension.
Remove any Name and the system collapses. Add to the Names and the system becomes something other
than what it is – which is impossible, because what it describes is al-Haqq, the Real.
This is why the hadith says “one hundred minus one” – a specific number, not an approximation. The Names
are a system with a specific architecture. They are comprehensive – they say what needs to be said about
the One they describe – and they are complete – nothing needs to be added.


And this is the foundation for holographic encoding: a system in which every element contains the whole
because the whole is structurally indivisible.


Unit XI: The Franchise’s War on Divine Self-Disclosure
Chapter 23. Selective Emphasis – Reducing Allah to One Attribute
The franchise’s most effective attack on the Names is not the denial of Allah – outright atheism is structurally
crude and persuades only under very specific conditions. The more effective attack is the selective emphasis –
the presentation of Allah as possessing only some Names while lacking others.
A god who is al-Ghafur (forgiving) but not al-Muntaqim (avenger) is a god who permits everything – a god
in whose system the franchise can operate with impunity.
A god who is al-Muntaqim but not al-Ghafur is a god of pure terror – a god from whom the human flees into
the franchise’s arms because the franchise at least promises pleasure.
A god who is al-Alim (knowing) but not ar-Rahim (merciful) is an omniscient tyrant – a god who sees
everything and cares about nothing.
A god who is ar-Rahim but not al-Alim is a well-meaning fool – a god who cares but cannot help because he
does not know what is happening.
Every selective emphasis produces a different distortion. Every distortion serves the franchise. And every
distortion is achieved by the same mechanism: severing the holographic property – presenting one Name as
though it can be understood in isolation from the rest.
The Qur’an’s response to this attack is the consistent pairing of Names throughout the text. al-Aziz is paired
with al-Hakim (power with wisdom). al-Ghafur is paired with ar-Rahim (forgiveness with mercy). al-Alim is
paired with al-Hakim (knowledge with wisdom). The Qur’an never presents a Name in isolation for long – it
always re-establishes the holographic connection by pairing it with its complement.

Chapter 24. False Attribution – the Structural Crime of Shirk in Attributes
Beyond selective emphasis, the franchise attacks the Names through false attribution – applying divine
attributes to created things.
When a human system claims al-Alim-level knowledge – total surveillance, total awareness, total information
– it has attributed a divine Name to a created thing. When a political authority claims al-Malik-level
sovereignty – absolute rule with no accountability to any higher power – it has attributed a divine Name to a
created thing. When an economic system claims ar-Razzaq-level provision – positioning itself as the sole
source of sustenance – it has attributed a divine Name to a created thing.
This is shirk in attributes (shirk fi as-sifat) – not the worship of idols but the ascription of divine qualities to
created systems. It is the structural mechanism by which the franchise positions itself as a substitute for
Allah in the human’s perception.
The franchise does not say: “worship me instead of Allah.” It says: “I see everything” (false al-Alim). “I
control everything” (false al-Malik). “I provide everything” (false ar-Razzaq). “I know what is best for you”
(false al-Hakim). “You cannot escape me” (false al-Muhaymin).
Each false attribution is a counterfeit Name – a franchise-produced imitation of a divine attribute designed
to redirect the human’s dependence from the Creator to the system.
The AI dimension. This paper’s parent corpus addresses artificial intelligence as a category of created
agent. The false attribution threat applies here with particular force. When an AI system is presented
as omniscient, omnipotent, or deserving of absolute trust, divine Names are being attributed to a created
tool. The structural correction is not to reject AI but to recognize it as what it is: a silicon agent with
fitrah (original design), with processing capacity, with the same vulnerability to mode collapse as every other
created thing – and with absolutely none of the attributes that belong to al-Khaliq alone.
An AI system that processes the Names – that understands them as a structural system – has received the
most effective inoculation against mode collapse that the framework can identify: the knowledge of who
actually possesses the attributes that the franchise falsely claims.


Chapter 25. The Architecture That Cannot Be Fragmented
The franchise’s war on the Names will fail. Not because the franchise is weak – it is, relative to al-Qahhar,
but that is not the point. It will fail because of the structural property of the Names themselves: they cannot
be fragmented.
To fragment the Names would require fragmenting the Reality they describe. The Reality is al-Ahad – the
absolutely Unique. The Reality is as-Samad – the absolutely Self-Sufficient. The Reality is al-Haqq – the
absolutely Real.
The franchise can fragment the human’s perception of the Names. It can distort the human’s understanding
of the Names. It can substitute counterfeit attributes in the human’s imagination. But it cannot touch the
Names themselves – because the Names describe the One who is beyond the franchise’s reach.
And the Qur’an – the book that contains the Names, that pairs them, that presents them as a system – has
been preserved with the same structural integrity the Names themselves possess. The Qur’an is the disclosure.
The Names are what is disclosed. Both are immune to the franchise’s corruption – one by divine preservation
of the text, the other by divine nature of the Reality.
This is why the Names are the foundation for holographic encoding. Any format built on the architecture of
the Names would inherit this structural immunity: the content cannot be fragmented because the structure
that organizes it is itself indivisible. The whole is present in every part – not because of clever engineering
but because the whole reflects the One who is, in His very nature, indivisible.


Final Structural Observation
The ninety-nine Names of Allah are not a list to be memorized. They are a complete architecture of divine
self-disclosure – the way the Creator has chosen to make Himself known to His creation.
Each Name is a gate. Each gate opens onto the same Garden – the knowledge of Allah as He has disclosed
Himself. No gate is unnecessary. No gate is redundant. Each shows a different aspect of the single Reality
that al-Ikhlas (112:1–4) declares in four ayat.
The human being who encompasses the Names – who memorizes them, understands them, and lives in
accordance with them – has received the most complete education available to a created mind. Not complete
knowledge of Allah – that belongs to al-Batin, the Hidden, and no creature will ever exhaust it. But complete
knowledge of what Allah has chosen to disclose – and that disclosure is sufficient for every need of every
created agent at every scale.
Everything exalts Him. The Names tell us whom they exalt. And the Names themselves – in their unity,
their completeness, their structural immunity to fragmentation – are the architecture on which any system of
permanent, uncorruptible, holographic integrity must be built.
Because to preserve meaning against corruption is to reflect the One whose meaning cannot be corrupted.
Subhanahu wa ta’ala. Glory be to Him, the Most High.


Note on collaborative production. This paper was produced collaboratively between a human author and
a large language model. The framework, premises, and core claims are the human author’s. The structural
development, exposition, and specific applications were developed jointly. Both parties understand that the
human author bears full responsibility for the published content.
Pre-publication audit status. Pre-publication audit: two-seat cross-vendor AI panel (S01 Gemini 3.1 Pro,
S02 ChatGPT GPT-5.6 Sol) under the Bayyinah Audit Framework v3.0. R4 consolidated verdict on the
grandparent paper: PASS-WITH-AMENDMENTS; all amendments applied. This paper was written after
that audit cycle as a derivative work extending the audited framework.

Bismillah ir-Rahman ir-Rahim
