# Paper 11: The Chants Have Cycled
*Structural Convergence Between The Mars Volta's Lyrical Corpus and the Qur'anic Architecture of Formed Agency*

**Bilal Syed Arfeen** with analytical synthesis by **Claude (Anthropic, Opus 4.6)**
*August 2026, v1.1*

---

### Collaboration Statement
This paper was developed collaboratively between Bilal Syed Arfeen (Project Leader) and Claude (Anthropic,
Opus 4.6, Researcher) across extended multi-session dialogue. Bilal Arfeen provided the foundational
interpretive direction, the theological architecture (drawn from two prior papers by the same author), the
directive to examine The Mars Volta’s lyrics through the lens of Qur’anic formed agency and computational
ontology, and the critical corrections that shaped the paper’s final posture. Claude provided the lyrical
analysis, the structural mapping between the two domains, the identification of convergence points and their
limits, and the articulation of confounds. The intellectual direction originated with Bilal Arfeen. The analysis
was collaborative. Bilal Arfeen takes responsibility for the paper’s claims.
An advisory peer review was conducted by ChatGPT (OpenAI), whose independent evaluation identified
factual errors, overclaim instances, a missing album in the declared corpus, and bibliography gaps. The v1.1
revision absorbs all confirmed findings. Cross-model verification is a methodological commitment of this
research program.
Claude is credited as a collaborative research tool in accordance with Anthropic’s Terms of Service. This
credit reflects the collaborative nature of the work and does not constitute a claim of independent authorship
by the AI system. All AI-generated contributions were directed, reviewed, and approved by the human
author.

### Abstract
This paper examines the lyrical corpus of The Mars Volta (seven studio albums, 2003–2022) through the
interpretive framework developed in two prior papers by the same author: a three-level architecture of
moral responsibility for formed agents (Arfeen, 2026a), and a computational ontology grounded in Islamic
monotheism (Arfeen & Claude, 2026b). The Mars Volta’s lyrics, written primarily by Cedric Bixler-Zavala
across stated inspirations ranging from a friend’s suicide to honour killings to Scientology, recapitulate
structural features the Qur’anic sources also describe: entity–partnership, generational transmission of corrupt
formations, beautification that makes the formed person suppose himself guided, the failure of sanctuary
when the threat is internal, and cyclical closure. The convergence is a Pillar 2 observation (convergence
with an independent domain) rather than a Pillar 1 claim (direct textual evidence), and the analysis is
exploratory rather than confirmatory. The paper does not claim that the lyrics confirm the theological
framework. It proposes that the structural resemblance is non-trivial, subject to the convergence-illusion risk
and the confounds identified in Section 6. The paper also documents how the analysis itself recapitulated a
retracted framework (recorded in Arfeen 2026a, Appendix B) before the replacement architecture corrected
it, and treats that self-correction as part of the result.
Keywords: formed agency, generational transmission, structural convergence, The Mars Volta, Qur’anic
exegesis, computational ontology, adversarial architecture, beautification, innate disposition, independent
articulation


### 1. Introduction
#### 1.1 The interpretive problem
The Mars Volta’s lyrics are among the most deliberately opaque in contemporary rock. Cedric Bixler-Zavala’s
writing is surrealist, densely metaphorical, and resistant to paraphrase. Across seven studio albums and two
decades, his stated inspirations include a fictionalised narrative drawing on the life and suicide of his friend
Julio Venegas (De-Loused in the Comatorium, 2003), a concept whose origin story was later acknowledged as
fabricated (Frances the Mute, 2005; see Rodriguez-Lopez & Bixler-Zavala, 2017), the abuse and fear of religion
(Amputechture, 2006), a ouija board purchased in Jerusalem (The Bedlam in Goliath, 2008), personal themes
of loss and relationship in a more restrained register (Octahedron, 2009), mythological and nursery-rhyme
sources (Noctourniquet, 2012), and his family’s legal battles with the Church of Scientology (The Mars Volta,
2022; see Beaumont-Thomas, 2022).
Despite these varied inspirations, a recurring structural pattern appears across the catalog: an entity that
partners with or inhabits a human host; a formation transmitted across generations that the inheritor did not
choose; a mechanism of beautification that makes the condition invisible to the person inside it; the failure of
sanctuary when the threat is already interior; and a cyclical structure that forecloses exit.
This paper asks why a similar pattern appears across albums with unrelated stated concepts, and proposes
that the pattern is recognisable because it resembles a real structural feature of the human condition that the
Qur’anic sources also describe. The Mars Volta did not derive their lyrics from the Qur’an. The convergence
is therefore evidence for the descriptive power of the proposed structure, not for the band’s theological
commitments.

#### 1.2 The framework applied
Two prior papers by this author supply the interpretive scaffold.
The first, Inheritance, Transmission, and Turning Back (Arfeen, 2026a), develops a three-level architecture
of moral responsibility for a person formed inside a tradition he did not choose. Level one (inherited position)
carries no culpability: the pen does not record against a child, and corruption of the innate disposition (fitrah)
is framed as an injury done to him rather than an act he performed. Level two (sustained participation after
the arrival of capacity and a conclusive proof) is where responsibility attaches. Level three (transmission, the
act of forming the next generation) is where weight attaches and compounds with the number who follow.
The paper treats both responsible species (mankind and the concealed ones, jinn) together, examines their
collaboration as a worked case of joint corruption with undivided responsibility, and establishes that the
remedy (turning back, tawbah) is indexed to the person’s present position rather than to reconstruction of
his causal history.
The second, Computational Tawhid (Arfeen & Claude, 2026b), proposes that reality is a divine computation;
that consciousness is fundamental to all entities within it; that the human is a process endowed with genuine
agency, comprehensive discrimination (the Names), and a pre-installed alignment toward truth (fitra); and
that the adversarial architecture described in the Qur’an operates as corruption within the computation
rather than as a rival creative force.

#### 1.3 What is claimed and what is not
Claimed: The Mars Volta’s lyrics independently articulate structural features that resemble those described
in the formed-agency architecture and the computational ontology, and the resemblance is specific enough to
be worth examining. The convergence is a Pillar 2 observation: it is suggestive rather than probative, and it
is subject to the convergence-illusion risk identified in the Computational Tawhid paper.
Not claimed: that Cedric Bixler-Zavala is encoding Qur’anic content, that the lyrics confirm the theological
framework, that the convergence establishes the framework’s truth, or that the lyrics are best read through
this lens rather than others. The paper presents one reading. It does not claim exclusivity.


### 2. Method and source handling
#### 2.1 Corpus
The corpus comprises the seven studio albums released by The Mars Volta between 2003 and 2022: De-Loused
in the Comatorium (2003), Frances the Mute (2005), Amputechture (2006), The Bedlam in Goliath (2008),
Octahedron (2009), Noctourniquet (2012), and The Mars Volta (2022). The acoustic re-recording Que Dios Te
Maldiga Mi Corazon (2023) and the ninth studio album Lucro Sucio; Los Ojos del Vacio (2025) fall outside
the analytical window and are not coded.

#### 2.2 Unit of analysis
The primary unit is the album. A motif is coded as present for an album if at least one song on the album
contains lyrical or structural features that correspond to the motif definition. A single song is sufficient for a
positive coding; absence requires that no song on the album contains such features. Ambiguous cases are
coded as ambiguous and noted.

#### 2.3 Motif definitions
Five motifs were identified during the analysis. They emerged from the lyrical review rather than being
pre-specified, and the study is therefore exploratory rather than confirmatory. A future confirmatory test
would require pre-registration of motifs and independent coding.
M1: Entity–partnership. An entity that inhabits, accompanies, merges with, or operates from within a
human figure’s perceptual field. The entity is not merely external; it shapes the host’s experience from the
inside.
M2: Generational transmission. The condition the protagonist occupies was not chosen by him. It was
inherited, received, or transmitted from a prior generation or a pre-existing arrangement.
M3: Beautification. The formed person does not know he is formed. The condition is invisible to the
person inside it, and the perceptual or evaluative apparatus that would detect it has itself been shaped by
the condition.
M4: Failed sanctuary. A space or institution that should offer protection fails because the threat is already
inside it.
M5: Cyclical closure. The condition repeats. The structure forecloses exit not by force but by returning
the person to the starting point.

#### 2.4 Source hierarchy
For artist statements: contemporary primary interviews and official liner material are preferred; later
recollections are secondary; secondary summaries (including Wikipedia-attributed statements) are tertiary
and flagged. For lyrics: official lyric sheets and licensed editions are preferred. Where unofficial transcriptions
are used, uncertainty is noted.

#### 2.5 What would count as a failed mapping
The catalog-wide recurrence claim would fail if any album in the corpus showed no positive coding on a
majority of the five motifs, or if a comparator corpus (unrelated to occult, institutional, or religious themes)
showed equal motif density under the same definitions.

### 3. The structural pattern across the catalog
#### 3.1 The recurring motifs
The following table summarises the motif coding across the seven albums. “+” indicates a positive coding
with at least one well-sourced textual instance. “~” indicates an ambiguous or attenuated presence. Evidence


for each coding is provided in the discussion below.

M2                   M3
Album              M1 Entity          Transmission         Beautification   M4 Sanctuary       M5 Cycle
De-Loused          +                  +                    +                +                  +
(2003)
Frances the        +                  +                    +                +                  +
Mute (2005)
Amputechture       +                  +                    +                +                  +
(2006)
Bedlam in          +                  +                    +                +                  +
Goliath (2008)
Octahedron         ~                  +                    ~                +                  +
(2009)
Noctourniquet      +                  +                    +                +                  +
(2012)
The Mars Volta     +                  +                    +                +                  +
(2022)


#### 3.2 Album-by-album evidence
De-Loused in the Comatorium (2003). M1: the Comatorium itself is an interior architecture inhabited
by forces that shape the protagonist’s experience from within. M2: the protagonist Cerpin Taxt did not
choose his coma; his condition was precipitated by self-destruction modelled on a real person’s history (Julio
Venegas, who entered a coma after a deliberate overdose, recovered, and later took his own life by jumping
from a bridge; see Bixler-Zavala in contemporary 2003 interviews). M3: the Comatorium presents itself as
real while the waking world does not. M4: recovery from the coma (the ostensible sanctuary of waking life)
leads directly to suicide. M5: the protagonist exits one death-state and enters another.
Frances the Mute (2005). M1: the spectral figures (Miranda, Cassandra, the Widow) mediate the
protagonist’s search and are not merely external. M2: the search is for biological parents, tracing a lineage
the protagonist did not select. M3: the album’s own origin story (the diary found in a repossessed car) was
acknowledged as fabricated in the documentary Omar and Cedric: If This Ever Gets Weird (Rodriguez-Lopez
& Bixler-Zavala, 2017), which is itself a beautification: the audience was given a false account and treated it
as real. M4: “Cassandra Gemini” places death imagery (morgues, sarcophagi) inside the space of preservation.
M5: the album’s final passage returns to the opening guitar motif, structurally closing the loop.
Amputechture (2006). M1: the Baphomet and the forces invoked in “Day of the Baphomets.” M2:
“Meccamputechture” references “humans as ornaments” and inherited practices. M3: the Baphomet is
worshipped as genuine by those who cannot distinguish it from the real; the album’s overriding theme is the
abuse and fear of religion (PopMatters, 2006). M4: the religious institution itself is the source of the harm it
should protect against. M5: the cyclical character is structural rather than narrative (each self-contained
song circles its own theme).
The Bedlam in Goliath (2008). M1: Goliath, described by Cedric as a composite of one male and two
female voices channelled through the ouija board (Bixler-Zavala, Mojo, February 2008). M2: the board’s
history predates the band; the entities have their own prior existence. M3: “Wax Simulacra” (the copy
replacing the original) and “Ilyena” (“can’t spot through the lens”). M4: “Ilyena” (“bleeding through your
sanctuary”). M5: the album’s production narrative is cyclical (the board kept producing the same entities
night after night, and the band’s attempts to complete the album kept being disrupted). Cedric stated that
the album was “one huge metaphor for the way women are treated in Islamic society (honour killings, etc.)
not just a story about a ouija board” (Bixler-Zavala, in a statement preserved in Octahedron-era interview
reporting; Westword, January 2008).
Octahedron (2009). This is the album where the motif coding is most attenuated, and the honest assessment
is that it provides weaker evidence for the pattern than the other six. M1 (ambiguous): “Teflon” contains


imagery of hostage-taking and assassination, and “Halo of Nembutals” names a halo composed of sedatives
(an entity-like presence operating through chemical means), but neither presents a clearly interior entity
in the way the other albums do. M2 (positive): “Since We’ve Been Wrong” opens the album with an
acknowledgment of sustained error inherited across time. M3 (ambiguous): Cedric described “Luciforms”
as evoking the Salem Witch Trials and satirising the religious right’s treatment of women, which implies a
critique of institutional beautification, but the beautification motif is less explicit in the lyrics themselves
than on other albums. M4 (positive): “Teflon” (“what am I without the bruises? / this switch, it won’t come
on”) depicts a condition where the damage has become constitutive of identity, so that the self is its own
failed sanctuary. M5 (positive): the album’s restraint is itself cyclical – a return to quiet after the chaos of
Bedlam, replicating the coma-to-waking-to-death cycle of De-Loused.
Noctourniquet (2012). M1: the Hyacinthus figure and the Solomonic addresses. M2: the Solomon
Grundy lifecycle (born on Monday, died on Saturday) treats an entire human life as a deterministic loop the
protagonist did not author. M3: the title itself (nocturnal + tourniquet) describes something that stops the
bleeding at night – a concealment of the wound. M4: “held under the water in a blacklist trough / buried
in the plots of your front steps” locates the threat inside the domestic space. M5: the Solomon Grundy
structure is the purest cycle in the catalog – a nursery-rhyme loop that begins and ends in the same place.
The Mars Volta (2022). M1: the Church of Scientology as an institutional entity operating from within
Cedric’s family life. M2: Cedric’s Scientology involvement was introduced through his wife, who was
recruited through a social network; the self-titled album documents the multi-year process of extraction.
M3: “Blacklight Shine” reveals what is invisible under normal light; the lyrics address karma, culprits, and
concealed operations (Bixler-Zavala, singing in Spanish and English; see Beaumont-Thomas, 2022). M4: the
Church is a sanctuary (a religious community) that is the source of the harm. M5: the album’s existence (a
reunion after a decade-long split caused in part by Scientology) is itself evidence of cyclical return.

#### 3.3 What the table shows and does not show
Six of seven albums show clear positive coding on all five motifs. Octahedron shows positive coding on three
motifs and ambiguous coding on two. The pattern is present across the catalog but is not uniformly strong.
The honest summary is that the structural recurrence is demonstrated across six albums with strong evidence
and across a seventh with partial evidence.
Two explanations remain live hypotheses, and the paper does not choose between them.
The first is artistic: Cedric has a set of thematic preoccupations that he projects onto whatever material he
is working with. This is ordinary for any artist with a consistent vision, and it does not require a theological
explanation. The seven albums are not independent observations; they share the same principal lyricist and
a long-running creative partnership, so cross-album recurrence is weaker evidence than recurrence across
genuinely independent artists or institutions.
The second is structural: the formed-agency architecture predicts that any sufficiently detailed artistic depiction
of institutional corruption will contain these features, because entity–partnership, inherited arrangement,
beautification, failed sanctuary, and cyclical closure are features of the phenomenon regardless of its local
instantiation. This prediction is testable in principle (by coding independent corpora against the same motif
definitions) but has not been tested in this study.

### 4. Case study: “Ilyena”
“Ilyena” is the third track on The Bedlam in Goliath (2008) and is selected as the primary case because it
condenses all five motifs into a single song and because Cedric’s stated intent for the song contradicts its
lyrical content in a way that is analytically productive.

#### 4.1 The name
Cedric stated that he named the song after Helen Mirren’s birth name (“Ilyena”) and described the song
as “a little lighthearted and different from the subject matter,” part of his stated attempt to counter the


male spirit by invoking figures such as Metatron, Ariel, and St. Gabriel (Bixler-Zavala, Marquee magazine,
January 2008). Within the album’s narrative, the name is also associated with one of the voices channelled
through the ouija board.
The name therefore operates on two registers: a protective naming strategy (invoking a real, powerful,
surviving woman) and the name of a figure within the demonological narrative. Under the formed-agency
architecture, this duality is structurally resonant: the same word can function as shield and wound because
the partnership itself is a condition in which protection and threat share a single locus.

#### 4.2 Structural mapping
The exhausted ritual cycle (M5). The song opens with chants that have cycled. Under the formed-agency
architecture, the cycling is the generational transmission reaching completion: the protocol has run its loop.
“How can I go wrong” reads as the deterministic confidence of a completed process.
The Edenic inversion (M2). “There will be no Eve for Adam / If the apples have gone.” Under the
architecture’s treatment of the innate disposition (fitrah), corruption of the default is an injury inflicted, not
a choice exercised. The apples being “gone” is not a transgression but an absence – a completed operation in
which the Edenic possibility has been foreclosed.
Incarnated debts and incarceration (M2, M1). “I need a brand new skin / Incarnated debts /
Incarcerated when the deeds are left to purchase.” Under the architecture, “incarnated debts” maps onto
level one: obligations that have taken flesh, arriving before the child could consent. “Incarcerated when the
deeds are left to purchase” describes being imprisoned before the transaction is complete. The song does not
resolve whether the speaker is the entity seeking a new host or the child describing his own condition. In the
cohabitation model the architecture describes, the two voices are indistinguishable from inside.
The chain of contamination (M1, M3). “Entity ingredients / A mass of blackened scapes begins.”
“Entity ingredients” treats the adversarial agent as assembled from components rather than naturally born,
which is consistent with the Computational Tawhid framework’s treatment of the adversarial process as
corruption within the computation. “Scapes” evokes the scapegoat mechanism, and the “mass” is both a
quantity and a ceremony.
The invitation into death. “Follow me to die / Follow me into oblivion.” Under the architecture, this
maps onto 14:22: the tempter’s authority extends to calling, not compelling. “Follow me” is an invitation.
The song does not resolve whether the person follows freely or under duress, and the architecture holds that
both conditions are real and differently weighted.
Concealment and failed sanctuary (M3, M4). “Can’t spot through the lens / Intention accident /
Bleeding through your sanctuary.” Under the architecture, the lens that cannot detect the condition has
been shaped by the formation (43:36–37: the companion bars from the path while the person supposes
himself guided). “Intention accident” compresses the structure of institutional concealment: intentional harm
classified as accidental. “Bleeding through your sanctuary” locates the violence inside the space of protection.

#### 4.3 The register mismatch as evidence
Cedric described “Ilyena” as “lighthearted” while its lyrics describe the dissolution of identity within an
inescapable ritual loop. The NME review noted the song was “creepy and unsettling” despite veering “close
to electropop danceability” (NME, 1 February 2008). The musical surface presents safety while the words
describe annihilation. Under the Computational Tawhid framework, this register mismatch is itself consistent
with the beautification mechanism: the arrangement presents itself as pleasant while operating as something
else.

### 5. The spectrum of participation
The analysis reveals a spectrum of participation rather than a single category of agent. This spectrum is the
interpretive feature that distinguishes the replacement framework from the withdrawn uniform-conspiracy
model recorded in Appendix B of the transmission paper.


#### 5.1 Equal partners
In “Day of the Baphomets” (Amputechture), agents are depicted as active collaborators: offering entrails,
born to bring death, planting vermin deliberately. Under the architecture, these are agents at level three.

#### 5.2 The coerced
In “Ilyena,” the entity’s request to “hide with me identity” and the bleeding through sanctuary describe a
condition in which resistance meets retaliation from within the person’s own perceptual field. Under the
architecture, 16:106 covers this case.

#### 5.3 The unknowing
In “Wax Simulacra,” the copy replaces the original and the person cannot tell the difference. In the self-
titled album, Cedric’s years inside Scientology are described in retrospect as genuine delusion. Under the
architecture, this maps onto 43:37: those barred from the path while supposing themselves guided.

#### 5.4 What the lyrics do not contain
The lyrics do not contain a remedy. Across seven albums and two decades, the catalog documents the
condition of being formed inside a corrupt arrangement and offers no instruction for what to do about it.
The architecture supplies what the lyrics lack: turning back (tawbah), indexed to present position, available
because the innate disposition persists beneath the formation at a level the formation cannot reach (30:30).
Whether this absence in the lyrics is diagnostic (evidence that observation alone cannot provide the exit) or
merely reflects artistic choice, the paper cannot determine. The observation is noted; the inference is not
drawn.

### 6. The self-correction
#### 6.1 What happened during the analysis
The initial interpretive exercise applied a framework in which most of the human race is covertly partnered
with adversarial entities. The withdrawn document (recorded in Arfeen 2026a, Appendix B) is described
there as positing a multi-generational deception involving lifelong induced states from infancy, a fabricated
history, a divinely protected minority persecuted by a deluded majority, and validation through persecution.
The framework mapped onto the Mars Volta lyrics with apparent precision.
The framework was then recognised as the same one withdrawn in Appendix B, for two reasons: it contradicted
itself (involuntary subjugation from infancy is incompatible with divine justice), and it had no remedy.
The replacement architecture, applied to the same lyrics, produced a different reading. The lyrics describe a
spectrum of participation rather than a uniform conspiracy. The inheritor’s defense is sincere rather than
performed. The beautification makes the person suppose himself guided, which is a different claim from
conscious performance. And the remedy is available because the fitrah persists beneath the formation.

#### 6.2 Why the self-correction matters
The episode demonstrates that the retracted framework can generate a coherent and initially persuasive
interpretation of this corpus. Its retraction is not because it fails to describe the observations but because it
fails structurally: it contradicts its own premises and offers no remedy. The replacement architecture describes
the same observations without those structural failures. One important test of a superseding framework is
whether it can preserve the predecessor’s explanatory successes while removing its structural defects.
The strongest reading of this episode is methodological: the same corpus can sustain a superficially precise
but structurally defective reading, so apparent precision of fit is not sufficient. That lesson directly reinforces
the convergence-illusion warning.


### 7. Confounds and limitations
#### 7.1 The convergence-illusion risk
The Computational Tawhid paper identifies this as the primary vulnerability of any framework that finds
structural convergence across independent domains. The analyst’s prior commitment to the Qur’anic
framework shapes which lyrical features are selected, how they are interpreted, and which mappings are
deemed significant. Complete objectivity is not claimed.

#### 7.2 Source independence is not analytical independence
The Mars Volta did not have access to the author’s theological framework. The source corpus is therefore
independent of the framework’s production. However, the analyst did have access to the framework, and
the mapping is not an independent test. Source independence protects against deliberate encoding or direct
influence. It does not protect against analyst-side confirmation bias. A genuinely independent test would
require pre-registered motif definitions and blind coding by independent raters.

#### 7.3 Corpus dependence
The seven albums share the same principal lyricist and a long-running creative partnership. Cross-album
recurrence within one artist’s corpus is weaker evidence than recurrence across genuinely independent artists
or institutions. A future stronger test would pre-specify the five motifs and score corpora produced by
independent creators.

#### 7.4 Feature dependence
The five motifs are not necessarily independent. Narratives about cults, institutions, abuse, inheritance,
or corrupt systems naturally tend to co-produce internalised control, concealment, failed protection, and
recurrence. A five-motif match may therefore contain fewer than five independent pieces of information.

#### 7.5 Motif-selection flexibility
The five motifs emerged during the analysis rather than being pre-specified. The study is therefore exploratory.
Metaphorical lyrics can often be coded into broad motif definitions in multiple ways, and a post-hoc motif set
cannot distinguish genuine structural convergence from flexible pattern-matching. The paper labels the study
accordingly.

#### 7.6 No comparator framework or negative control
The paper acknowledges that Jungian, Marxist, or Gnostic readings might also produce plausible mappings.
Neither an alternative framework nor a negative-control corpus (one not selected for occult, institutional, or
religious themes) has been tested. The absence of a comparator limits the specificity claim.

#### 7.7 The fabrication of origin stories
Cedric and Omar acknowledged in a documentary that the backstory for Frances the Mute was fabricated
(Rodriguez-Lopez & Bixler-Zavala, 2017). Retrospective artist explanations may differ across time and
promotional contexts. The source hierarchy in Section 2.4 addresses this, but the reader should note that
artist-supplied narratives are not treated as ground truth.

#### 7.8 Lyric-source provenance
The analysis turns on specific phrases. Where official lyric sheets were unavailable, unofficial transcriptions
were used with the understanding that transcription errors are possible. Uncertainty is noted in the case
study where exact wording matters.


### 8. Conclusion
Three levels of claim should be distinguished.
Observed within this analysis. Five structural motifs (entity–partnership, generational transmission,
beautification, failed sanctuary, cyclical closure) can be mapped across six of seven albums in the Mars Volta
corpus with clear positive coding, and across the seventh with partial evidence. The motifs emerged during
the analysis and were not pre-specified.
Interpretive claim. These motifs resemble features of the formed-agency architecture developed in the
companion papers: the three-level structure of moral responsibility, the partnership between mankind and the
concealed ones, the beautification mechanism at 43:36–37, the failed sanctuary when the threat is internal, and
the cyclical closure of generational transmission. The resemblance is specific enough to be worth examining
and is subject to the confounds identified in Section 7.
Framework claim. The formed-agency architecture supplies a remedy (turning back, tawbah) and an
ontological ground (the persistence of the innate disposition beneath the formation, 30:30) that the lyrics do
not contain. This is a claim of the applied architecture, not a result established by the Mars Volta analysis.
The convergence between the lyrical corpus and the theological framework is suggestive. It is not probative.
The analysis is exploratory, the confounds are real, and a confirmatory test would require pre-registered motifs,
independent coding, and a comparator corpus. What the analysis demonstrates is that the structural features
described in the formed-agency architecture are independently recognisable in a corpus produced without
access to the framework. Whether that recognisability reflects shared structural origin, common features
of institutional-corruption narratives, or the analyst’s pattern-detection apparatus imposing coherence on
independent artistic decisions is a question the analysis raises and does not resolve.

# Paper 12: Islamic Prayers I
*Master Cheat Sheet: 100 Invocations Against Magic, Jinn, and Evil*

**Bilal Syed Arfeen**

---

Master Cheat Sheet: 100 Invocations
Against Magic, Jinn, and Evil
    This specialized document contains 100 powerful protective verses
    (Ayat) and supplications (Duas) strictly focused on Ruqyah Ash-
    Shar'iyyah—the Islamic practice of spiritual defense. Every
    invocation is provided with its complete transliteration for direct
    recitation, targeted at banishing satans, warding off the evil eye,
    breaking sorcery (sihr), and shielding against human and jinn
    oppressors.

### 1. The Ultimate Shield (Al-Fatihah)

    Bismillahir-rahmanir-rahim. Alhamdulillahi rabbil-'alamin. Ar-
    rahmanir-rahim. Maliki yawmid-din. Iyyaka na'budu wa iyyaka
    nasta'in. Ihdinas-siratal-mustaqim. Siratal-ladhina an'amta
    'alayhim, ghayril-maghdubi 'alayhim wa lad-dallin.


Purpose: The foundational cure (Ruqyah) against all spiritual ailments and magic.

### 2. The Master Verse of Protection (Ayatul Kursi, Baqarah 2:255)

    Allahu la ilaha illa Huwa, Al-Hayyul-Qayyum. La ta'khudhuhu
    sinatun wa la nawm, lahu ma fis-samawati wa ma fil-'ard. Man
    dhal-ladhi yashfa'u 'indahu illa bi-idhnihi. Ya'lamu ma bayna
    aydihim wa ma khalfahum, wa la yuhituna bi shai'im-min 'ilmihi illa
    bima sha'a. Wasi'a kursiyuhus-samawati wal-'ard, wa la ya'uduhu
    hifzhuhuma, wa Huwal-'Aliyyul-'Azim.


Purpose: Burns away demonic presence and establishes an angelic guard against jinn.

### 3. The Seals of Al-Baqarah (2:285-286)

    Amanar-rasulu bima unzila ilayhi mir-rabbihi wal-mu'minun... La
    yukallifullahu nafsan illa wus'aha... Anta mawlana fansurna 'alal-
    qawmil-kafirin.


Purpose: Nullifies the plots of evil and protects the household from satans for the night.
### 4. Surah Al-Ikhlas (112)

    Bismillahir-rahmanir-rahim. Qul huwallahu ahad. Allahus-samad.
    Lam yalid wa lam yulad. Wa lam yakul-lahu kufuwan ahad.


Purpose: Purifies faith and deflects unseen attacks through the absolute oneness of Allah.

### 5. Surah Al-Falaq (113)

    Bismillahir-rahmanir-rahim. Qul a'udhu bi rabbil-falaq. Min sharri
    ma khalaq. Wa min sharri ghasiqin idha waqab. Wa min sharrin-
    naffathati fil-'uqad. Wa min sharri hasidin idha hasad.


Purpose: Directly breaks the knots of sorcerers, dark magic, and the evil eye of the envious.

### 6. Surah An-Nas (114)

    Bismillahir-rahmanir-rahim. Qul a'udhu bi rabbin-nas. Malikin-nas.
    Ilahin-nas. Min sharril-waswasil-khannas. Alladhi yuwaswisu fi
    sudurin-nas. Minal-jinnati wan-nas.


Purpose: Extracts and banishes the whisperings of both human and jinn devils from the
chest.

### 7. Breaking Sihr: Al-Baqarah (2:102)

    Wat-taba'u ma tatlush-shayatinu 'ala mulki sulayman, wa ma
    kafara sulaymanu wa lakin-nash-shayatina kafaru yu'allimunan-
    nasas-sihr...


Purpose: The primary verse recited in Ruqyah to dismantle black magic and expose
demonic contracts.

### 8. Nullifying Magic: Al-A'raf (7:117-119)

    Wa awhayna ila musa an alqi 'asaka fa idha hiya talqafu ma
    ya'fikun. Fawaqa'al-haqqu wa batala ma kanu ya'malun. Faghulibu
    hunalika wanqalabu saghirin.


Purpose: Destroys illusions and active sorcery, rendering the work of magicians entirely
void.
### 9. Invalidating Sorcerers: Yunus (10:81-82)

    Falamma alqaw qala musa ma ji'tum bihis-sihru innallaha
    sayubtiluh, innallaha la yuslihu 'amalal-mufsidin. Wa
    yuhiqqullahul-haqqa bikalimatihi wa law karihal-mujrimun.


Purpose: A powerful declaration that Allah will swiftly obliterate any magic brought forth
by evildoers.

### 10. Defeating Magicians: Taha (20:69)

    Wa alqi ma fi yaminika talqaf ma sana'u, innama sana'u kaydu
    sahirin wa la yuflihus-sahiru haythu ata.


Purpose: Declares the ultimate failure of the sorcerer, regardless of the demonic power they
summon.

### 11. Repelling Jinn: Al-Mu'minun (23:97-98)

    Wa qul rabbi a'udhu bika min hamazatish-shayatin. Wa a'udhu bika
    rabbi an yahdurun.


Purpose: A strict command forbidding devils from approaching or influencing your physical
space.

### 12. Banishing Rebellious Demons: As-Saffat (37:1-7)

    Was-saffati saffa. Faz-zajirati zajra. Fat-taliyati dhikra... Wa hifzan
    min kulli shaytanin marid.


Purpose: Strikes down flying jinn and obstinate demons (Marid) attempting to eavesdrop
on the heavens.

### 13. Overpowering Jinn: Ar-Rahman (55:33-35)

    Ya ma'sharal jinni wal insi inistata'tum an tanfudhu min aqtaris
    samawati wal ardi fanfudhu... Yursalu 'alaykuma shuwazun min
    narin wa nuhasun fala tantasiran.


Purpose: A stern warning to Jinn that they cannot escape Allah's authority, threatening
them with a flame of fire.
### 14. Exorcising Entities: Al-Hashr (59:21-24)

    Law anzalna hadhal-qur'ana 'ala jabalin lara'aytahu khashi'an
    mutasaddi'an min khashyatillah...


Purpose: So heavy with divine presence that it forces possessive jinn to flee or be crushed.

### 15. Deflecting the Evil Eye: Al-Qalam (68:51-52)

    Wa in yakadulladhina kafaru layuzlikunaka bi'absarihim lamma
    sami'udh-dhikra wa yaquluna innahu lamajnun. Wa ma huwa illa
    dhikrun lil-'alamin.


Purpose: Used specifically to cure illnesses caused by malicious envy and the lethal "evil
eye".

### 16. Divine Sufficiency: Al-Baqarah (2:137)

    Fasayakfikahumullahu wa huwas-sami'ul-'alim.


Purpose: "Allah will suffice you against them." A razor-sharp defense against plotting
enemies.

### 17. Turning Fear into Safety: Al-Imran (3:173)

    Hasbunallahu wa ni'mal wakil.


Purpose: The ultimate shield against overwhelming odds and massive alliances of human or
jinn enemies.

### 18. Entrusting Affairs: Ghafir (40:44-45)

    Wa ufawwidu amri ilallah, innallaha basirun bil-'ibad.
    Fawaqahullahu sayyi'ati ma makaru...


Purpose: Deflects the secret plots and traps set by sorcerers and hostile humans.

### 19. The Invisible Barrier: Al-Isra (17:45)

    Wa idha qara'tal-qur'ana ja'alna baynaka wa baynal-ladhina la
    yu'minuna bil-akhirati hijaban mastura.


Purpose: Veils the reciter from the sight and perception of wicked humans and evil spirits.
### 20. The Wall of Blindness: Yasin (36:9)

    Wa ja'alna min bayni aydihim saddan wa min khalfihim saddan
    fa'aghshaynahum fahum la yubsirun.


Purpose: Casts a barrier before and behind you, rendering enemies completely blind to your
presence.

### 21. Protection from Oppressors: Yunus (10:85-86)

    Fa qalu 'alallahi tawakkalna rabbana la taj'alna fitnatan lil-
    qawmiz-zalimin. Wa najjina birahmatika minal-qawmil-kafirin.


Purpose: A plea to not be made a victim or trial for oppressive, dark-hearted people.

### 22. Rescue from Darkness: Al-Anbiya (21:87)

    La ilaha illa anta subhanaka inni kuntu minaz-zalimin.


Purpose: The prayer of Yunus to break out of suffocating spiritual darkness and demonic
distress.

### 23. Curing Black Magic (The Water Ruqyah)

    Recite Fatihah, Ayatul Kursi, Ikhlas, Falaq, Nas, and the verses of
    Sihr (7:117, 10:81, 20:69) over water and drink/bathe.


Purpose: The standard prophetic protocol for physically washing away the effects of a
sorcerer's spell.

### 24. Seeking Immediate Refuge (Ta'awwudh)

    A'udhu billahi minash-shaytanir-rajim.


Purpose: The instant, primary weapon against any sudden flash of demonic whispering,
doubt, or anger.

### 25. Refuge of the Perfect Words

    A'udhu bikalimatillahit-tammati min sharri ma khalaq.


Purpose: Recited 3 times. Places an impenetrable shield against evil creatures, jinn, and
pests in any location.
### 26. The Name that Harms Nothing

    Bismillahilladhi la yadurru ma'a-smihi shay'un fil-ardi wa la fis-
    sama'i wa huwas-sami'ul-'alim.


Purpose: Recited 3 times morning/evening. Blocks all magic, poison, and sudden afflictions.

### 27. The Comprehensive Shield

    A'udhu bikalimatillahit-tammati min ghadabihi wa 'iqabihi, wa
    sharri 'ibadihi, wa min hamazatish-shayatini wa an yahdurun.


Purpose: Protects from Allah's wrath, evil humans, and the physical approach of demons
(used for night terrors).

### 28. Ruqyah of Jibril

    Bismillahi arqik, min kulli shay'in yu'dhik, min sharri kulli nafsin aw
    'ayni hasidin. Allahu yashfik, bismillahi arqik.


Purpose: The angelic exorcism to cure diseases born of the evil eye or demonic touch.

### 29. Complete Eradication of Pain

    Bismillah (3x), A'udhu billahi wa qudratihi min sharri ma ajidu wa
    uhadhir (7x).


Purpose: Placed over a hurting body part to dispel pain caused by physical injury or
spiritual attack.

### 30. Banishing the Devil of Prayer (Khinzab)

    A'udhu billahi minash-shaytanir-rajim (then dry spit to the left 3
    times).


Purpose: Used mid-prayer when a specific demon (Khinzab) tries to steal focus and ruin
worship.

### 31. Exiting the House (Angelic Guard)

    Bismillahi tawakkaltu 'alallahi, wa la hawla wa la quwwata illa
    billah.
Purpose: Angels take over your protection, and Satan is forced to physically distance
himself from you.

### 32. Entering the House (Locking out Jinn)

    Bismillahi walajna, wa bismillahi kharajna, wa 'ala Rabbina
    tawakkalna.


Purpose: Forces demons to stay outside; they cannot sleep or eat in a home entered with
this dhikr.

### 33. Entering the Restroom (Impurity Shield)

    Bismillah. Allahumma inni a'udhu bika minal-khubthi wal-
    khaba'ith.


Purpose: Blocks male and female jinn who dwell in impure places from seeing your
nakedness or harming you.

### 34. Protection Before Intimacy

    Bismillah, Allahumma jannibnash-shaytana, wa jannibish-shaytana
    ma razaqtana.


Purpose: Prevents Satan from sharing in the union and protects the unborn child from
demonic interference.

### 35. Shield Against Nightmares

    A'udhu bikalimatillahit-tammati min ghadabihi wa 'iqabihi... wa
    min hamazatish-shayatini wa an yahdurun.


Purpose: Breaks the psychological attacks and terrifying visions sent by demons during
sleep.

### 36. Upon Waking from a Bad Dream

    Spit drily to the left three times, seek refuge in Allah from Satan, and
    change your sleeping side.


Purpose: The prophetic physical action to severe the connection of a demonic dream.
### 37. Protection Against Tyrants

    Allahumma inna naj'aluka fi nuhurihim wa na'udhu bika min
    shururihim.


Purpose: A tactical spiritual maneuver: placing Allah directly between you and an
oppressive human enemy.

### 38. Silencing Hostile Humans

    Allahummakfinihim bima shi't.


Purpose: "Suffice me against them however You will." Asks Allah to personally dispatch
your attackers.

### 39. Entering a New or Abandoned Place

    A'udhu bikalimatillahit-tammati min sharri ma khalaq.


Purpose: Ensures the local jinn or hidden dangers of that specific land cannot harm you.

### 40. When an Animal or Vehicle Stumbles

    Bismillah.


Purpose: Instead of cursing (which makes Satan swell with pride), this shrinks him to the
size of a fly.

### 41. Hearing a Dog Bark or Donkey Bray at Night

    A'udhu billahi minash-shaytanir-rajim.


Purpose: These animals vocalize because they literally see demons roaming; this dua
protects the hearer.

### 42. Cupped Hands Bedtime Ruqyah

    Recite Ikhlas, Falaq, Nas, blow into cupped hands, and wipe the
    entire body.


Purpose: Creates an aura of physical and spiritual protection that lasts until dawn.
### 43. Surrendering the Soul at Night

    Allahumma aslamtu wajhi ilayk, wa fawwadtu amri ilayk...


Purpose: Protects the soul while it travels during sleep, preventing jinn from capturing or
tormenting it.

### 44. Dua against the Dajjal & Hell

    Allahumma inni a'udhu bika min 'adhabi jahannam, wa min
    'adhabil-qabr, wa min fitnatil-mahya wal-mamat, wa min sharri
    fitnatil-masihid-dajjal.


Purpose: The ultimate shield against the greatest deceiver (Dajjal) and the tortures of the
unseen.

### 45. Erasing the Evil Eye from Oneself

    Ma sha' Allah, la quwwata illa billah.


Purpose: Used when admiring your own wealth, family, or body to prevent cursing yourself
with your own eye.

### 46. Blessing Others to Prevent Hasad

    Allahumma barik 'alayh (or Barakallahu fik).


Purpose: The mandatory phrase when admiring someone else, neutralizing any accidental
evil eye you might cast.

### 47. Prayer of Deliverance from Enemies

    Allahumma munzilal-kitab, sari'al-hisab, ihzimil-ahzab.
    Allahummahzimhum wa zalzilhum.


Purpose: "O Revealer of the Book, Defeat the coalitions, defeat them and shake them." Used
against overwhelming human forces.

### 48. Seeking Forgiveness to Break Curses

    Astaghfirullahal-'Azimal-ladhi la ilaha illa huwal-Hayyul-Qayyum,
    wa atubu ilayh.
Purpose: Sins create vulnerabilities in one's spiritual armor; deep istighfar repairs these
breaches.

### 49. Protection of Children from Magic & Jinn

    U'idhukuma bikalimatillahit-tammah, min kulli shaytanin wa
    hammah, wa min kulli 'aynin lammah.


Purpose: The specific ruqyah used by the Prophet on his grandsons to deflect demons,
venomous pests, and the evil eye.

### 50. Overcoming Heavy Demonic Oppression

    La ilaha illallahul-'Azimul-Halim, la ilaha illallahu
    Rabbul-'Arshil-'Azim, la ilaha illallahu Rabbus-samawati wa
    Rabbul-ardi wa Rabbul-'Arshil-Karim.


Purpose: The Dua of Distress; lifts the heavy, suffocating pressure placed on the chest by
dark magic.

### 51. Unbreaking the Heart from Sorcery

    Ya Hayyu ya Qayyum, bi-rahmatika astaghith. Aslih li sha'ni
    kullahu, wa la takilni ila nafsi tarfata 'ayn.


Purpose: Begs the Ever-Living to take control of your spiritual state and repair damage
done by sorcerers.

### 52. Protection from Demonic Laziness (Ghaflah)

    Allahumma inni a'udhu bika minal-hammi wal-hazan, wal-'ajzi wal-
    kasal, wal-bukhli wal-jubn, wa dala'id-dayni wa ghalabatir-rijal.


Purpose: Removes the specific lethargy and despair injected into the mind by jinn to stop
worship.

### 53. Defeating Doubts (Waswasah) in Faith

    Amantu billahi wa rusulih.


Purpose: Cuts off the demonic whispers that try to inject logical fallacies or doubts about
Allah.
### 54. Deflecting Illusions

    Huwal-awwalu wal-akhiru waz-zahiru wal-batinu, wa huwa bikulli
    shay'in 'alim.


Purpose: Recited to ground the mind in ultimate truth when satans try to distort reality or
cause hallucinations.

### 55. Ward against the Jinn of the Market

    La ilaha illallahu wahdahu la sharika lah, lahul-mulku wa lahul-
    hamdu, yuhyi wa yumitu, wa huwa hayyun la yamutu, bi yadihil-
    khayru, wa huwa 'ala kulli shay'in qadir.


Purpose: Markets are the battlegrounds of devils; this creates a massive spiritual fortress
amidst chaos.

### 56. Protecting Food from Satan

    Bismillahi awwalahu wa akhirahu.


Purpose: If you forget Bismillah, this makes Satan vomit whatever he stole from your meal.

### 57. Closing the Doors at Night

    Bismillah (while closing doors, covering vessels).


Purpose: Satan cannot open a door or uncover a vessel that has been sealed with the Name
of Allah.

### 58. Nullifying Bad Omens (Tiyarah)

    Allahumma la tayra illa tayruk, wa la khayra illa khayruk, wa la
    ilaha ghayruk.


Purpose: Destroys the psychological magic of superstition, shutting a door Satan uses to
cause fear.

### 59. Expiating Gatherings from Evil Whispers

    Subhanakallahumma wa bihamdika, ash-hadu an la ilaha illa anta,
    astaghfiruka wa atubu ilayk.
Purpose: Wipes away any sins or demonic influence generated through gossip or idle talk in
a group.

### 60. Prayer for Light against Darkness

    Allahummaj'al fi qalbi nuran, wa fi lisani nuran, wa fi sam'i nuran,
    wa fi basari nuran...


Purpose: Saturates the practitioner with divine light, making it agonizing for dark jinn to
remain near them.

### 61. Leaving the Mosque to Face the World

    Allahumma inni as'aluka min fadlik, Allahumma'simni minash-
    shaytanir-rajim.


Purpose: A specific request for a shield against Satan upon returning to worldly affairs and
business.

### 62. Supplication Against the Evil of the Wind

    Allahumma inni as'aluka khayraha, wa a'udhu bika min sharriha.


Purpose: Jinn often travel in whirlwinds; this dua protects against the destructive elements
hidden in storms.

### 63. Neutralizing Anger (The Fire of Iblis)

    Allahummaghfir li dhanbi, wa adh-hib ghayza qalbi, wa a'idhni
    minash-shaytan.


Purpose: Anger is Satan's playground; this explicitly asks Allah to extinguish that demonic
fire.

### 64. The Ten Verses of Al-Baqarah (1-4, 255, 284-286)

    Alif-lam-mim. Dhalikal-kitabu la rayba fih... (and the rest of the
    sequence).


Purpose: Reciting these specific 10 verses in a house guarantees that Satan will not enter it
that night.
### 65. Breaking Ties with Demonic Pacts

    Qul ya ayyuhal-kafirun. La a'budu ma ta'budun...


Purpose: Surah Al-Kafirun. A total disavowal of false deities and dark spirits, severing all
spiritual compromises.

### 66. Reciting Al-Mulk (67)

    Tabarakal-ladhi bi yadihil-mulku wa huwa 'ala kulli shay'in qadir...


Purpose: A continuous shield against the punishment of the grave and the terrifying entities
within it.

### 67. Seeking Allah's Awe over Enemies

    Allahumma inna naj'aluka fi nuhurihim wa na'udhu bika min
    shururihim.


Purpose: Places the awe and terror of Allah into the hearts of human oppressors and
magical practitioners.

### 68. Guarding the Morning (Until Evening)

    La ilaha illallahu wahdahu la sharika lah, lahul-mulku wa lahul-
    hamdu, wa huwa 'ala kulli shay'in qadir. (100 times).


Purpose: Provides a fortress against Satan for the entire day, equating to freeing 10 slaves
in reward.

### 69. Erasing the Scars of Sorcery

    Allahumma Rabban-nas, adh-hibil-ba's, washfi, antash-Shafi, la
    shifa'a illa shifa'uk, shifa'an la yughadiru saqaman.


Purpose: The primary dua for healing any physical or spiritual ailment left behind by black
magic.

### 70. Exposing Hidden Magic

    Hasbiyallahu wa ni'mal-wakil. (Repeated continuously).


Purpose: Draws Allah's direct intervention to expose hidden amulets, buried sihr, or the
identity of the sorcerer.
### 71. Protection from Sudden Calamity

    Bismillahilladhi la yadurru... (3x).


Purpose: Ensures no sorcerer's spell can suddenly take effect and no jinn can strike without
warning.

### 72. Repelling the Jinn of the House ('Ammar)

    As-salamu 'alayna wa 'ala 'ibadillahis-salihin.


Purpose: Saying this upon entering an empty house ensures safety from the neutral/hostile
jinn residing there.

### 73. Acknowledging Allah's Decree against Sihr

    Qaddarallahu wa ma sha'a fa'al.


Purpose: Reminds the victim that even magic only harms by Allah's permission, shutting
down despair.

### 74. The Call to Prayer (Athan)

    Allahu Akbar, Allahu Akbar...


Purpose: Satan flees violently from the sound of the Athan; it is used as a powerful Ruqyah
to clear a home.

### 75. The Iqamah (Call to Commence)

    Allahu Akbar... Qad qamatis-salah...


Purpose: Further drives away demons who attempt to return after the initial Athan has
finished.

### 76. Shield of the Righteous (Al-Kahf 18:39)

    Wa lawla idh dakhalta jannataka qulta ma sha' Allahu la quwwata
    illa billah.


Purpose: Specifically protects one's property, gardens, and wealth from the destructive evil
eye.
### 77. Guarding the Eyes and Ears

    Allahumma 'afini fi badani, Allahumma 'afini fi sam'i, Allahumma
    'afini fi basari. La ilaha illa anta.


Purpose: Blocks jinn from manipulating your senses, causing hallucinations, or inducing
magical deafness/blindness.

### 78. The Overwhelming Defense (Al-Baqarah 2:250)

    Rabbana afrigh 'alayna sabran wa thabbit aqdamana wansurna
    'alal-qawmil-kafirin.


Purpose: The prayer of Talut (Saul) against Goliath, used to hold ground against
overwhelming evil forces.

### 79. Breaking Demonic Pride

    Subhanallah, walhamdulillah, wa la ilaha illallah, wallahu akbar.


Purpose: The sheer weight of these words crushes the arrogance of possessing spirits
during an exorcism.

### 80. Surah At-Tariq (86:1-17)

    Was-sama'i wat-tariq... Innahum yakiduna kayda, wa akidu kayda.
    Famahhilil-kafirina amhilhum ruwayda.


Purpose: Confirms that Allah's plot outmaneuvers the secret, dark plots of the sorcerers.

### 81. Nullifying Envy (Al-Nisa 4:54)

    Am yahsudunan-nasa 'ala ma atahumullahu min fadlih...


Purpose: Recited to break the specific jealousy and resentment that fuels the dark energy of
the evil eye.

### 82. Banishing Fear of the Unseen

    La tahzan innallaha ma'ana.


Purpose: "Do not be sad/afraid, indeed Allah is with us." The words of the Prophet in the
cave, repelling absolute dread.
### 83. Destroying Witchcraft Knots

    Wa min sharrin-naffathati fil-'uqad. (Repeated frequently).


Purpose: Specifically targets the knots blown upon by witches, dissolving the binding spells.

### 84. Expelling Jinn from the Body

    Ukhruj 'aduwwallah.


Purpose: "Come out, enemy of Allah!" The prophetic command used directly against a
possessing entity.

### 85. The Fire of Ruqyah (Al-Mulk 67:5)

    Wa laqad zayyannas-sama'ad-dunya bimasabiha wa ja'alnaha
    rujuman lish-shayatin...


Purpose: Reminds the jinn that they are stoned with flaming meteors by the angels.

### 86. Fortifying the Mind

    Allahumma inni a'udhu bika min zawali ni'matik, wa tahawwuli
    'afiyatik, wa fuja'ati niqmatik, wa jami'i sakhatik.


Purpose: Protects against the sudden stripping of sanity or health caused by intense dark
magic.

### 87. Surah Al-Jinn (72:1-3)

    Qul uhiya ilayya annahustama'a nafarun minal-jinni fa qalu inna
    sami'na qur'anan 'ajaba...


Purpose: Recited to remind hostile jinn of the righteous among their own kind who
submitted to the Quran.

### 88. The Supplication of Nuh against Evil

    Rabbi la tadhar 'alal-ardi minal-kafirina dayyara.


Purpose: Invoked when wicked humans or sorcerers become so utterly corrupt they must
be entirely removed.
### 89. Prayer for Absolute Purity

    Allahumma tahhir qalbi wa hassin farji.


Purpose: Protects against the incubus/succubus jinn that attempt to induce illicit desires or
sleep paralysis.

### 90. Sealing the House (Surah Al-Baqarah)

    Iqra'u suratal-baqarah, fa inna akhdhaha barakatun wa tarkaha
    hasratun wa la tastati'uhal-batalah.


Purpose: "The sorcerers cannot overpower Surah Al-Baqarah." Playing or reciting it
completely clears a home.

### 91. Defeating Oppressive Magic (Al-A'raf 7:120-122)

    Wa ulqiyas-saharatu sajidin. Qalu amanna birabbil-'alamin. Rabbi
    musa wa harun.


Purpose: Reminds the demons that even the greatest magicians of Pharaoh surrendered to
Allah's power.

### 92. Seeking Refuge from Human Devils

    A'udhu billahi minal-jinnati wan-nas.


Purpose: Focusing on the end of An-Nas, explicitly targeting the human practitioners of
magic and evil.

### 93. Purging Poison and Sihr

    Eating 7 Ajwa Dates in the morning.


Purpose: A physical/spiritual Sunnah defense: "Whoever eats 7 Ajwa dates in the morning,
neither magic nor poison will harm him that day."

### 94. Seeking Refuge for a Dwelling

    A'udhu bikalimatillahit-tammati min sharri ma khalaq.


Purpose: When stepping into a hotel, new house, or forest, this prevents the resident jinn
from bothering you.
### 95. Breaking Generational Curses

    Rabbana waghfir lana wa li'ikhwaninal-ladhina sabaquna bil-iman
    wa la taj'al fi qulubina ghillan lilladhina amanu...


Purpose: Clears the bloodline and heart of lingering malice that dark entities exploit.

### 96. Shield against the Whispers of Death

    Allahumma inni a'udhu bika an yatakhabbatanish-shaytanu 'indal-
    mawt.


Purpose: Specific protection against Satan appearing at the moment of death to steal one's
faith.

### 97. Extinguishing the Sorcerer's Fire

    Hasbunallahu wa ni'mal-wakil.


Purpose: Just as it cooled the fire for Ibrahim, it cools the burning sensation associated with
black magic in the body.

### 98. Unveiling Deception

    Allahumma arinal-haqqa haqqan warzuqnat-tiba'ah, wa arinal-
    batila batilan warzuqnaj-tinabah.


Purpose: Allows the victim to see through the illusions and gaslighting created by sorcerers
and human devils.

### 99. Surah Az-Zalzalah (99)

    Idha zulzilatil-ardu zilzalaha...


Purpose: Used in Ruqyah to violently shake the spiritual foundation of possessing entities,
forcing them out.

### 100. The Ultimate Return

    Inna lillahi wa inna ilayhi raji'un.


Purpose: Destroys the goal of the sorcerer (which is to cause despair) by returning all
sovereignty and trust to Allah.

Bismillah ir-Rahman ir-Rahim

Bismillah ir-Rahman ir-Rahim

# Paper 13: Islamic Prayers II
*Master Cheat Sheet: 100 Islamic Protection Invocations (Full Transliteration)*

**Bilal Syed Arfeen**

---

Master Cheat Sheet: 100 Islamic
Protection Invocations (Full
Transliteration)
    This expanded document contains 100 powerful protective verses
    (Ayat) and supplications (Duas) from the Quran and Sunnah. Every
    invocation is provided with its complete transliteration so it can be
    recited directly from this document, followed by its purpose for
    victory over evil, protection from the Hellfire, the Dajjal, and
    comprehensive daily spiritual defense.

### 1. Al-Fatihah (The Opening)

    Bismillahir-rahmanir-rahim. Alhamdulillahi rabbil-'alamin. Ar-
    rahmanir-rahim. Maliki yawmid-din. Iyyaka na'budu wa iyyaka
    nasta'in. Ihdinas-siratal-mustaqim. Siratal-ladhina an'amta
    'alayhim, ghayril-maghdubi 'alayhim wa lad-dallin.


Purpose: The greatest Surah, used as a cure (Ruqyah) and fundamental spiritual shield.

### 2. Ayatul Kursi (Baqarah 2:255)

    Allahu la ilaha illa Huwa, Al-Hayyul-Qayyum. La ta'khudhuhu
    sinatun wa la nawm, lahu ma fis-samawati wa ma fil-'ard. Man
    dhal-ladhi yashfa'u 'indahu illa bi-idhnihi. Ya'lamu ma bayna
    aydihim wa ma khalfahum, wa la yuhituna bi shai'im-min 'ilmihi illa
    bima sha'a. Wasi'a kursiyuhus-samawati wal-'ard, wa la ya'uduhu
    hifzhuhuma, wa Huwal-'Aliyyul-'Azim.


Purpose: The greatest verse for absolute protection; keeps a guardian angel over you at
night.

### 3. Baqarah 2:285-286

    Amanar-rasulu bima unzila ilayhi mir-rabbihi wal-mu'minun... La
    yukallifullahu nafsan illa wus'aha... Anta mawlana fansurna 'alal-
    qawmil-kafirin.


Purpose: The last two verses of Baqarah. Reciting them at night suffices against all evil.
### 4. Al-Ikhlas (112)

    Bismillahir-rahmanir-rahim. Qul huwallahu ahad. Allahus-samad.
    Lam yalid wa lam yulad. Wa lam yakul-lahu kufuwan ahad.


Purpose: Equals one-third of the Quran; ultimate declaration of monotheism against
polytheistic evil.

### 5. Al-Falaq (113)

    Bismillahir-rahmanir-rahim. Qul a'udhu bi rabbil-falaq. Min sharri
    ma khalaq. Wa min sharri ghasiqin idha waqab. Wa min sharrin-
    naffathati fil-'uqad. Wa min sharri hasidin idha hasad.


Purpose: The primary shield against external evils: envy, witchcraft, and hidden harms.

### 6. An-Nas (114)

    Bismillahir-rahmanir-rahim. Qul a'udhu bi rabbin-nas. Malikin-nas.
    Ilahin-nas. Min sharril-waswasil-khannas. Alladhi yuwaswisu fi
    sudurin-nas. Minal-jinnati wan-nas.


Purpose: The primary shield against internal evils: the whisperings of Satans and men.

### 7. Yasin 36:9

    Wa ja'alna min bayni aydihim saddan wa min khalfihim saddan
    fa'aghshaynahum fahum la yubsirun.


Purpose: Creates a spiritual barrier or blind spot against enemies and attackers.

### 8. Yasin 36:58

    Salamun qawlan min rabbin rahim.


Purpose: Invokes direct peace and safety from the Lord of Mercy.

### 9. Ar-Rahman 55:33

    Ya ma'sharal jinni wal insi inistata'tum an tanfudhu min aqtaris
    samawati wal ardi fanfudhu, la tanfudhuna illa bisultan.


Purpose: Declares Allah's supreme boundary over Jinn and men; none can escape His
dominion.
### 10. Al-Kahf 18:10

    Idh awal-fityatu ilal-kahfi fa qalu rabbana atina min ladunka
    rahmatan wa hayyi' lana min amrina rashada.


Purpose: The prayer of the Cave Sleepers for physical safety and right guidance.

### 11. Al-Kahf 18:1-3 (Shortened opening)

    Alhamdulillahi ladhi anzala 'ala 'abdihil kitaba wa lam yaj'al lahu
    'iwaja...


Purpose: Reciting the opening verses of Kahf guarantees protection from the fitnah of the
Dajjal.

### 12. Al-Mu'minun 23:97-98

    Wa qul rabbi a'udhu bika min hamazatish-shayatin. Wa a'udhu bika
    rabbi an yahdurun.


Purpose: Direct command seeking refuge from the whisperings and presence of devils.

### 13. As-Saffat 37:7

    Wa hifzan min kulli shaytanin marid.


Purpose: A protective seal against rebellious and obstinate demonic forces.

### 14. At-Tariq 86:4

    In kullu nafsin lamma 'alayha hafiz.


Purpose: Affirmation that a guardian angel is appointed over every soul.

### 15. Al-Qalam 68:51

    Wa in yakadulladhina kafaru layuzlikunaka bi'absarihim lamma
    sami'udh-dhikra wa yaquluna innahu lamajnun.


Purpose: Traditional recitation to deflect the evil eye and malicious envy.
### 16. At-Tawbah 9:129

    Fa in tawallaw faqul hasbiyallahu la ilaha illa hu, 'alayhi
    tawakkaltu wa huwa rabbul 'arshil 'azim.


Purpose: Recited 7 times; Allah will suffice the reciter against whatever causes them grief.

### 17. Al-Hashr 59:22-24

    Huwallahulladhi la ilaha illa hu, 'alimul-ghaybi wash-shahadati
    huwar-rahmanur-rahim...


Purpose: Invokes the magnificent Names of Allah; brings angels to pray for your protection.

### 18. At-Tahrim 66:6

    Ya ayyuhal-ladhina amanu qu anfusakum wa ahlikum naran...


Purpose: A command and invocation to protect oneself and one's family from the Hellfire.

### 19. Al-Imran 3:8

    Rabbana la tuzigh qulubana ba'da idh hadaytana wa hab lana min
    ladunka rahmatan, innaka antal-wahhab.


Purpose: Protection against the heart deviating after being guided.

### 20. Al-Imran 3:173

    Hasbunallahu wa ni'mal wakil.


Purpose: Sufficient for us is Allah, and He is the best disposer of affairs. Recited in extreme
fear.

### 21. Yunus 10:81

    Falamma alqaw qala musa ma ji'tum bihis-sihru innallaha
    sayubtiluh, innallaha la yuslihu 'amalal-mufsidin.


Purpose: Musa's declaration to nullify dark magic and sorcery.
### 22. Taha 20:68-69

    Qulna la takhaf innaka antal-a'la. Wa alqi ma fi yaminika talqaf ma
    sana'u...


Purpose: Victory over illusions and the tricks of magicians/satans.

### 23. Al-Anbiya 21:87

    La ilaha illa anta subhanaka inni kuntu minaz-zalimin.


Purpose: The prayer of Yunus in the whale; rescues believers from deep distress and
darkness.

### 24. Ghafir 40:44

    Fasatadhkuruna ma aqulu lakum, wa ufawwidu amri ilallah,
    innallaha basirun bil-'ibad.


Purpose: Entrusting one's affairs entirely to Allah against the plots of enemies.

### 25. Shield of Allah's Name

    Bismillahilladhi la yadurru ma'a-smihi shay'un fil-ardi wa la fis-
    sama'i wa huwas-sami'ul-'alim.


Purpose: Recited 3x. Nothing in earth or heaven can harm the reciter.

### 26. Perfect Words of Allah

    A'udhu bikalimatillahit-tammati min sharri ma khalaq.


Purpose: Recited 3x in the evening. Complete protection from created evils and pests.

### 27. The Master Supplication of Forgiveness

    Allahumma anta Rabbi la ilaha illa anta, khalaqtani wa ana
    'abduka, wa ana 'ala 'ahdika wa wa'dika mastata'tu...


Purpose: Sayyid al-Istighfar. Guarantees Paradise if one dies that day/night.
### 28. Seeking Well-being

    Allahumma inni as'alukal-'afwa wal-'afiyata fid-dunya wal-
    akhirah... Allahummahfazni min bayni yadayya, wa min khalfi, wa
    'an yamini, wa 'an shimali, wa min fawqi...


Purpose: A massive shield for physical, spiritual, and family well-being against all directions
of attack.

### 29. Protection against unexpected affliction

    A'udhu bikalimatillahit-tammati min ghadabihi wa 'iqabihi, wa
    sharri 'ibadihi, wa min hamazatish-shayatini wa an yahdurun.


Purpose: Used when waking up in terror or suffering nightmares to repel demonic
presence.

### 30. Refuge from the 4 Trials

    Allahumma inni a'udhu bika min 'adhabi jahannam, wa min
    'adhabil-qabr, wa min fitnatil-mahya wal-mamat, wa min sharri
    fitnatil-masihid-dajjal.


Purpose: The mandatory pre-tasleem prayer against Hell, the Grave, Life/Death trials, and
the Dajjal.

### 31. Deliverance from Hellfire

    Allahumma ajirni minan-nar.


Purpose: Recited 7 times after Fajr and Maghrib. If you die, you are spared the Fire.

### 32. The Good of Both Worlds

    Rabbana atina fid-dunya hasanatan wa fil-akhirati hasanatan wa
    qina 'adhaban-nar.


Purpose: The most frequent dua of the Prophet to secure goodness and block the Hellfire.

### 33. Protection of the Face from Fire

    Allahumma inni as'aluka ridaaka wal-jannah, wa a'udhu bika min
    sakhatika wan-nar.
Purpose: Seeking Allah's pleasure and Paradise, and refuge from His wrath and the Fire.

### 34. Entering the Restroom

    Bismillah. Allahumma inni a'udhu bika minal-khubthi wal-
    khaba'ith.


Purpose: Protection from male and female devils in places of impurity.

### 35. Leaving the House

    Bismillahi tawakkaltu 'alallahi, wa la hawla wa la quwwata illa
    billah.


Purpose: Angels respond: 'You are guided, defended, and protected,' and Shaytan steps
away.

### 36. Entering the House

    Bismillahi walajna, wa bismillahi kharajna, wa 'ala Rabbina
    tawakkalna.


Purpose: Keeps Shaytan from finding shelter or food in your home.

### 37. Before Intercourse

    Bismillah, Allahumma jannibnash-shaytana, wa jannibish-shaytana
    ma razaqtana.


Purpose: Protects any resulting offspring from the harm of Satan.

### 38. During Anger

    A'udhu billahi minash-shaytanir-rajim.


Purpose: Extinguishes the fire of anger stoked by Iblis.

### 39. Hearing a Dog or Donkey at Night

    A'udhu billahi minash-shaytanir-rajim.


Purpose: Because animals can see devils that humans cannot.
### 40. When Stopping at a New Place

    A'udhu bikalimatillahit-tammati min sharri ma khalaq.


Purpose: Nothing will harm you in that land until you depart from it.

### 41. Against an Oppressor/Enemy

    Allahumma inna naj'aluka fi nuhurihim wa na'udhu bika min
    shururihim.


Purpose: O Allah, we place You before them and seek refuge in You from their evils.

### 42. When Overwhelmed

    Ya Hayyu ya Qayyum, bi-rahmatika astaghith. Aslih li sha'ni
    kullahu, wa la takilni ila nafsi tarfata 'ayn.


Purpose: Calling upon the Ever-Living to rectify all affairs and not leave you to yourself.

### 43. Against Debt and Coercion

    Allahumma inni a'udhu bika minal-hammi wal-hazan, wal-'ajzi wal-
    kasal, wal-bukhli wal-jubn, wa dala'id-dayni wa ghalabatir-rijal.


Purpose: Refuge from anxiety, sorrow, weakness, laziness, cowardice, debt, and the
subjugation of men.

### 44. Victory in Battle/Debate

    Hasbunallahu wa ni'mal-wakil.


Purpose: The words of Ibrahim in the fire and Muhammad at Uhud. Turning fear into safety.

### 45. Dua of Dawud for Strength

    Allahumma inni as'aluka hubbaka wa hubba man yuhibbuka,
    wal-'amalal-ladhi yuballighuni hubbak.


Purpose: Prayer for divine love and strength to do good deeds that lead to victory.
### 46. The Bedtime Cupped Hands

    Bismillahir-rahmanir-rahim. Qul huwallahu ahad... Bismillahir-
    rahmanir-rahim. Qul a'udhu bi rabbil-falaq... Bismillahir-rahmanir-
    rahim. Qul a'udhu bi rabbin-nas... (Recite all three fully, blow into
    hands, wipe over body).


Purpose: The Prophet's nightly ritual to physically wipe away evil from head to toe.

### 47. The Name to Die Upon

    Bismika Allahumma amutu wa ahya.


Purpose: In Your Name, O Allah, I die and I live. Secures the soul during the 'minor death' of
sleep.

### 48. Surrender of the Soul

    Allahumma aslamtu wajhi ilayk, wa fawwadtu amri ilayk, wa alja'tu
    zahri ilayk, raghbatan wa rahbatan ilayk, la malja'a wa la manja
    minka illa ilayk. Amantu bikitabikal-ladhi anzalt, wa binabiyyikal-
    ladhi arsalt.


Purpose: The ultimate nightly surrender of the soul to its Maker.

### 49. Jibril's Ruqyah

    Bismillahi arqik, min kulli shay'in yu'dhik, min sharri kulli nafsin aw
    'ayni hasidin. Allahu yashfik, bismillahi arqik.


Purpose: The specific exorcism/healing prayer the angel Jibril used on the Prophet.

### 50. Pain Relief & Defense

    Bismillah (3x), A'udhu billahi wa qudratihi min sharri ma ajidu wa
    uhadhir (7x).


Purpose: To banish physical pain and the evil attached to it.

### 51. Waking Up

    Alhamdulillahil-ladhi ahyana ba'da ma amatana wa ilayhin-nushur.


Purpose: Praising Allah for returning the soul and protecting it during sleep.
### 52. Wearing Clothes

    Alhamdulillahil-ladhi kasani hadhath-thawba wa razaqanihi min
    ghayri hawlin minni wa la quwwatin.


Purpose: Acknowledgment that protection and provision come entirely from Allah's power,
not our own.

### 53. Wearing New Clothes

    Allahumma lakal-hamdu, anta kasanitah, as'aluka min khayrihi wa
    khayri ma suni'a lah, wa a'udhu bika min sharrihi wa sharri ma
    suni'a lah.


Purpose: Invokes the good of the garment and wards off any evil associated with its making.

### 54. Leaving the Toilet

    Ghufranaka.


Purpose: Asking forgiveness, having left a place of impurity where the name of Allah is not
mentioned.

### 55. Starting Wudu

    Bismillah.


Purpose: Initiating purification in the Name of Allah, guarding the act from Satan.

### 56. Finishing Wudu

    Ash-hadu an la ilaha illallahu wahdahu la sharika lah, wa ash-hadu
    anna Muhammadan 'abduhu wa rasuluh. Allahummaj'alni minat-
    tawwabina waj'alni minal-mutatahhirin.


Purpose: Opens the eight gates of Paradise; secures spiritual and physical purity.

### 57. Going to the Mosque

    Allahummaj'al fi qalbi nuran, wa fi lisani nuran, wa fi sam'i nuran,
    wa fi basari nuran...


Purpose: A profound prayer for light, protecting the senses and the heart from darkness and
misguidance.
### 58. Entering the Mosque

    Bismillah, was-salatu was-salamu 'ala rasulillah. Allahummaftah li
    abwaba rahmatik.


Purpose: Seeking the opening of Allah's gates of mercy in His sanctuary.

### 59. Leaving the Mosque

    Bismillah was-salatu was-salamu 'ala rasulillah, Allahumma inni
    as'aluka min fadlik, Allahumma'simni minash-shaytanir-rajim.


Purpose: Asking for Allah's bounty in the world while seeking protection from Satan upon
re-entering worldly affairs.

### 60. Hearing the Athan

    Allahumma Rabba hadhihid-da'watit-tammah, was-salatil-qa'imah,
    ati Muhammadanil-wasilata wal-fadhilah, wab'ath-hu maqaman
    mahmudanil-ladhi wa'adtah.


Purpose: Guarantees the intercession of the Prophet on the Day of Judgment.

### 61. When in Distress

    La ilaha illallahul-'Azimul-Halim, la ilaha illallahu
    Rabbul-'Arshil-'Azim, la ilaha illallahu Rabbus-samawati wa
    Rabbul-ardi wa Rabbul-'Arshil-Karim.


Purpose: The Dua of Distress (Dua al-Karb) to lift overwhelming burdens and remove
unseen pressures.

### 62. Facing an Enemy (Alternative)

    Allahumma inna naj'aluka fi nuhurihim wa na'udhu bika min
    shururihim.


Purpose: Placing Allah as a shield directly in front of enemies to neutralize their plots.

### 63. Fear of a Specific Group

    Allahummakfinihim bima shi't.
Purpose: "O Allah, suffice me against them however You will." Total reliance on Allah's
defense mechanism.

### 64. Doubt in Faith (Waswasah)

    Amantu billahi wa rusulih.


Purpose: "I believe in Allah and His Messengers." Dispels the Satanic whisperings of doubt
concerning faith.

### 65. Dealing with a Difficult Task

    Allahumma la sahla illa ma ja'altahu sahlan, wa anta taj'alul-hazna
    idha shi'ta sahlan.


Purpose: Removes the "evil" or barrier of impossibility and makes difficult tasks easy.

### 66. Protection of Children

    U'idhukuma bikalimatillahit-tammah, min kulli shaytanin wa
    hammah, wa min kulli 'aynin lammah.


Purpose: The supplication Ibrahim used for Ishaq and Isma'il, and Muhammad used for
Hasan and Husayn.

### 67. Visiting the Sick

    La ba'sa tahurun in sha' Allah.


Purpose: A declaration that the sickness will cleanse sins, protecting the sick from despair.

### 68. Visiting the Sick (7 times)

    As'alullahal-'Azima Rabbal-'Arshil-'Azimi an yashfiyak.


Purpose: If recited 7 times for a sick person whose time has not yet come, Allah will cure
them.

### 69. Eradicating a Bad Omen

    Allahumma la tayra illa tayruk, wa la khayra illa khayruk, wa la
    ilaha ghayruk.
Purpose: A shield against superstition, affirming that all destiny and power lies with Allah
alone.

### 70. Boarding a Vehicle/Mount

    Bismillah. Alhamdulillah. Subhanal-ladhi sakh-khara lana hadha
    wa ma kunna lahu muqrinin. Wa inna ila Rabbina lamunqalibun.


Purpose: Protects travelers from the dangers of the road by acknowledging Allah's
subjugation of the transport.

### 71. Entering a Town or Village

    Allahumma rabbas-samawatis-sab'i wa ma azlalna, wa rabbal-
    aratinas-sab'i wa ma aqlalna... as'aluka khayra hadhihil-qaryati wa
    khayra ahliha, wa a'udhu bika min sharriha wa sharri ahliha.


Purpose: Secures the good of a new settlement and shields against the evil of its inhabitants
or hidden harms.

### 72. Entering the Market

    La ilaha illallahu wahdahu la sharika lah, lahul-mulku wa lahul-
    hamdu, yuhyi wa yumitu, wa huwa hayyun la yamutu, bi yadihil-
    khayru, wa huwa 'ala kulli shay'in qadir.


Purpose: Provides massive spiritual reward and protection in a place often filled with
deception and heedlessness.

### 73. Unhappiness with Decree

    Qaddarallahu wa ma sha'a fa'al.


Purpose: "Allah has decreed, and what He wills, He does." Closes the door to Satan's "what

### 74. Seeing Someone in Affliction

    Alhamdulillahil-ladhi 'afani mim-mabtalaka bihi wa faddalani 'ala
    kathirin mimman khalaqa tafdila.


Purpose: Secures the reciter against ever being struck by that specific affliction or trial.
### 75. When the Wind Blows

    Allahumma inni as'aluka khayraha wa a'udhu bika min sharriha.


Purpose: Seeking the benefit of the wind (rain/pollen) and refuge from its destructive

### 76. Hearing Thunder

    Subhanal-ladhi yusabbihur-ra'du bihamdihi wal-mala'ikatu min
    khifatih.


Purpose: Protection against the terror and strike of lightning, honoring the awe of the

### 77. Fear of Shirk (Polytheism)

    Allahumma inni a'udhu bika an ushrika bika wa ana a'lam, wa
    astaghfiruka lima la a'lam.


Purpose: A vital shield against hidden polytheism (like showing off), which nullifies good
deeds.

### 78. Returning from Travel

    A'ibuna, ta'ibuna, 'abiduna, li-Rabbina hamidun.


Purpose: Repentant return, ensuring the traveler brings home blessings rather than the

### 79. Hearing a Rooster at Night

    Allahumma inni as'aluka min fadlik.


Purpose: Roosters see angels; this invocation asks for grace while heavenly beings are
present.

### 80. Placing Deceased in Grave

    Bismillahi wa 'ala sunnati rasulillah.


Purpose: Protects the deceased by formally placing them under the banner of the Prophet's
tradition.
### 81. Visiting Graves

    Assalamu 'alaykum ahlal-diyari minal-mu'minina wal-muslimin, wa
    inna in sha' Allahu bikum lahiqun. Nas'alullaha lana wa
    lakumul-'afiyah.


Purpose: Grants peace to the dead and seeks well-being (immunity from trials) for the living
and the deceased.

### 82. When Tragedy Strikes

    Inna lillahi wa inna ilayhi raji'un. Allahumma'jurni fi musibati
    wakhluf li khayran minha.


Purpose: Shields the heart from bitterness and guarantees Allah will replace the loss with
something better.

### 83. Seeing the Crescent Moon

    Allahu Akbar. Allahumma ahillahu 'alayna bil-amni wal-iman, was-
    salamati wal-islam, wat-tawfiqi lima tuhibbu wa tarda. Rabbuna wa
    Rabbukallah.


Purpose: Secures the coming month in peace, faith, safety, and submission to Allah.

### 84. Breaking the Fast

    Dhahabaz-zama'u wabtallatil-'uruqu, wa thabatal-ajru in sha'
    Allah.


Purpose: Thirst is gone, veins are moistened, and the reward is secured.

### 85. Before Eating (Forgot Bismillah)

    Bismillahi awwalahu wa akhirahu.


Purpose: If one forgets Bismillah, this retroactively prevents Satan from sharing the meal.

### 86. After Eating

    Alhamdulillahil-ladhi at'amani hadha wa razaqanihi min ghayri
    hawlin minni wa la quwwatin.
Purpose: A prayer of gratitude that forgives past sins and attributes all sustenance to Allah's
power alone.

### 87. Entering the Morning

    Allahumma bika asbahna wa bika amsayna, wa bika nahya wa bika
    namutu wa ilaykan-nushur.


Purpose: Secures the day under the authority and protection of Allah.

### 88. Entering the Evening

    Allahumma bika amsayna wa bika asbahna, wa bika nahya wa bika
    namutu wa ilaykal-masir.


Purpose: Secures the night under the authority and protection of Allah.

### 89. Repelling Distraction in Prayer

    A'udhu billahi minash-shaytanir-rajim (then dry spit to the left 3
    times).


Purpose: Directly banishes "Khinzab," the specific devil assigned to distract from prayer.

### 90. Experiencing Doubt

    Huwal-awwalu wal-akhiru waz-zahiru wal-batinu, wa huwa bikulli
    shay'in 'alim.


Purpose: "He is the First and the Last, the Ascendant and the Intimate, and He is, of all
things, Knowing." Cures profound spiritual doubts.

### 91. During a Windstorm

    Allahumma inni as'aluka khayraha, wa khayra ma fiha, wa khayra
    ma ursilat bih, wa a'udhu bika min sharriha, wa sharri ma fiha, wa
    sharri ma ursilat bih.


Purpose: A more comprehensive shield during extreme weather and storms.

### 92. When Angry (Alternative)

    Allahummaghfir li dhanbi, wa adh-hib ghayza qalbi, wa a'idhni
    minash-shaytan.
Purpose: "O Allah, forgive my sin, remove the anger in my heart, and protect me from
Satan."

### 93. Protection from Sudden Affliction

    Bismillahilladhi la yadurru... (Reciting it with absolute certainty).


Purpose: Repeating the Name of Allah 3x guarantees protection from sudden, unforeseen
paralysis or calamity.

### 94. When Fearing a Ruler/Authority

    Allahumma rabbas-samawatis-sab'i wa rabbal-'arshil-'azim, kun li
    jaran min fulan ibni fulan...


Purpose: A shield against tyrants, asking Allah to be a neighbor/protector against their
oppression.

### 95. Waking up in the Night

    La ilaha illallahu wahdahu la sharika lah... Subhanallah,
    walhamdulillah, wa la ilaha illallah, wallahu akbar, wa la hawla wa
    la quwwata illa billah.


Purpose: Whoever recites this upon waking suddenly, their subsequent prayer or dua is
guaranteed to be answered.

### 96. Fearing the Evil Eye on Oneself/Wealth

    Ma sha' Allah, la quwwata illa billah.


Purpose: "As Allah wills, there is no power except with Allah." Prevents one from
accidentally cursing their own blessings.

### 97. Committing a Sin

    Perform Wudu, pray 2 Rakahs, and say: Astaghfirullah.


Purpose: The immediate physical and spiritual protocol to erase a sin before it stains the
heart.

### 98. When an Animal Stumbles

    Bismillah.
Purpose: Saying this rather than cursing the animal prevents Satan from growing in size and
pride.

### 99. Supplication for the Host

    Allahumma barik lahum fima razaqtahum, waghfir lahum
    warhamhum.


Purpose: Blessing the host who fed you, protecting their home and provisions.

### 100. The Seal of the Gathering

    Subhanakallahumma wa bihamdika, ash-hadu an la ilaha illa anta,
    astaghfiruka wa atubu ilayk.


Purpose: The Expiation of Assembly (Kaffarat al-Majlis). Erases any idle talk, sin, or evil
generated during a gathering.

Bismillah ir-Rahman ir-Rahim
